The system has the potential to shape future research and to be extended beyond deepfake detection, but the limitations temper the overall impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### OpenNav: Open-World Navigation with Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18033v1)
- **Authors**: Mingfeng Yuan, Letian Wang, Steven L. Waslander
- **Abstract**: Pre-trained large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong common-sense reasoning abilities, making them promising for robotic navigation and planning tasks. However, despite recent progress, bridging the gap between language descriptions and actual robot actions in the open-world, beyond merely invoking limited predefined motion primitives, remains an open challenge. In this work, we aim to enable robots to interpret and decompose complex language instructions, ultimately synthesizing a sequence of trajectory points to complete diverse navigation tasks given open-set instructions and open-set objects. We observe that multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) exhibit strong cross-modal understanding when processing free-form language instructions, demonstrating robust scene comprehension. More importantly, leveraging their code-generation capability, MLLMs can interact with vision-language perception models to generate compositional 2D bird-eye-view value maps, effectively integrating semantic knowledge from MLLMs with spatial information from maps to reinforce the robot's spatial understanding. To further validate our approach, we effectively leverage large-scale autonomous vehicle datasets (AVDs) to validate our proposed zero-shot vision-language navigation framework in outdoor navigation tasks, demonstrating its capability to execute a diverse range of free-form natural language navigation instructions while maintaining robustness against object detection errors and linguistic ambiguities. Furthermore, we validate our system on a Husky robot in both indoor and outdoor scenes, demonstrating its real-world robustness and applicability. Supplementary videos are available at https://trailab.github.io/OpenNav-website/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "OpenNav: Open-World Navigation with Multimodal Large Language Models" introduces a novel framework for robot navigation in open-world environments using Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). OpenNav allows a robot to follow free-form language instructions and navigate using sensor data, effectively handling open-set objects and instructions without relying on in-context examples or pre-trained skills. The system integrates an open-vocabulary perception system (OVPS) with MLLMs for enhanced scene comprehension and uses a trajectory optimizer to generate geometry-compliant paths. The approach is validated through experiments on autonomous vehicle datasets (AVDs) and a physical robot in both indoor and outdoor settings, demonstrating robustness against object detection errors and linguistic ambiguities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper claims its primary novelty is directly generating trajectories using an MLLM for outdoor navigation with open-set instructions and objects, without relying on in-context examples, pre-trained skills, or motion primitives. This is a strong claim, and the described system offers a tangible advancement. The integration of a multi-expert system (MLLM + OVPS) to enhance robotic scene comprehension using a single task-agnostic prompt is also a novel contribution.
    -   **Strengths:** The zero-shot approach offers a significant advantage over methods relying on in-context learning, which often limits generalization. The code-generation capability of the MLLM, combined with classical planning techniques, represents a synergistic approach that leverages the strengths of both human-like reasoning and geometry-compliant planning.
    -   **Weaknesses:** While the paper demonstrates success on AVDs and a Husky robot, the complexity of real-world environments can present further challenges. The performance of OpenNav heavily relies on the OVPS, which may struggle in scenarios with low visibility or noisy sensor data. The paper also does not sufficiently elaborate on computational costs or real-time performance considerations, crucial for practical deployment. Additionally, there is limited discussion on the failure modes beyond object detection accuracy. How does the system handle situations when the robot loses localization, or when unexpected dynamic objects appear? A deeper analysis of the limitations and potential failure points would increase the robustness of the contribution. The comparison to LLM-TG focuses primarily on specific vs. ambiguous objects. Additional comparison to other contemporary methods in VLN and embodied AI (even if in simulation) could further strengthen the position of the approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper's potential significance lies in bridging the gap between high-level language understanding and low-level robot motion control in unstructured, open-world environments. By enabling robots to interpret complex instructions and synthesize trajectories dynamically, OpenNav could contribute to more intuitive and flexible human-robot interaction in real-world applications.
    -   **Strengths:**  The use of large-scale autonomous vehicle datasets for validation showcases the practicality and applicability of the framework in real-world settings. The successful execution of diverse free-form navigation instructions highlights the potential for increased robot autonomy and versatility.

*   **Justification for Score:**
    - OpenNav advances zero-shot VLN by integrating OVPS and MLLMs without in-context learning. This offers a substantial advancement over other methods.
    - The experimental validation is sound, but limited in scope, using only one AVD for the AVD experiments and lacking a robust ablation study.
    - Important limitations regarding localization robustness, computational cost, and handling of dynamic obstacles are not adequately addressed.
    - As such, the work shows promise, but further development and exploration of limitations is necessary.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Removing Box-Free Watermarks for Image-to-Image Models via Query-Based Reverse Engineering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18034v1)
- **Authors**: Haonan An, Guang Hua, Hangcheng Cao, Zhengru Fang, Guowen Xu, Susanto Rahardja, Yuguang Fang
- **Abstract**: The intellectual property of deep generative networks (GNets) can be protected using a cascaded hiding network (HNet) which embeds watermarks (or marks) into GNet outputs, known as box-free watermarking. Although both GNet and HNet are encapsulated in a black box (called operation network, or ONet), with only the generated and marked outputs from HNet being released to end users and deemed secure, in this paper, we reveal an overlooked vulnerability in such systems. Specifically, we show that the hidden GNet outputs can still be reliably estimated via query-based reverse engineering, leaking the generated and unmarked images, despite the attacker's limited knowledge of the system. Our first attempt is to reverse-engineer an inverse model for HNet under the stringent black-box condition, for which we propose to exploit the query process with specially curated input images. While effective, this method yields unsatisfactory image quality. To improve this, we subsequently propose an alternative method leveraging the equivalent additive property of box-free model watermarking and reverse-engineering a forward surrogate model of HNet, with better image quality preservation. Extensive experimental results on image processing and image generation tasks demonstrate that both attacks achieve impressive watermark removal success rates (100%) while also maintaining excellent image quality (reaching the highest PSNR of 34.69 dB), substantially outperforming existing attacks, highlighting the urgent need for robust defensive strategies to mitigate the identified vulnerability in box-free model watermarking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the vulnerability of box-free watermarking techniques used to protect deep generative networks (GNets). While these techniques aim to safeguard intellectual property by embedding watermarks into GNet outputs through a cascaded hiding network (HNet) encapsulated within a black-box operation network (ONet), the authors demonstrate that the hidden GNet outputs can be reliably estimated through query-based reverse engineering. They propose two attack methods: first, reverse-engineering an inverse model for HNet, and second, leveraging the additive property of the watermarking process to create a forward surrogate model of HNet. Both attacks successfully remove watermarks while maintaining good image quality, highlighting a critical vulnerability in existing box-free watermarking systems. The authors also discuss potential defense mechanisms and demonstrate their attack's effectiveness on image processing and generation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of this work lies in identifying and exploiting a previously overlooked vulnerability in box-free watermarking systems. While box-free watermarking has been considered a robust approach, the authors effectively demonstrate its susceptibility to query-based reverse engineering. The two attack methods, particularly the one exploiting the additive property, offer a novel perspective on circumventing these watermarking schemes. The concept of using specialized crafted queries to bypass the GNet function in order to isolate and reveal the watermarking mechanism employed by the HNet is also a key contribution. This is a significant advancement because it directly challenges the assumption that black-box encapsulation provides sufficient security.

*   **Significance:** The findings have significant implications for the security of intellectual property in deep learning models. The demonstrated vulnerability underscores the need for more robust defensive strategies in watermarking systems. The paper effectively demonstrates the limitations of current box-free watermarking and encourages further research into more secure and resilient methods. The provided analysis and potential mitigation strategies (API detection) contribute to a deeper understanding of the security landscape of deep learning models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clearly Defined Problem:** The paper clearly defines the problem of vulnerability in box-free watermarking.
    *   **Well-Explained Methodology:** The proposed attacks are well-explained, with clear descriptions of the underlying principles and implementation details.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the attacks in both image processing and generation tasks, backed by quantitative metrics and qualitative examples.
    *   **Practical Threat Model:** The threat model is realistic, considering a black-box API setting, which makes the findings practically relevant.
    *   **Potential Mitigation:** The discussion of API detection as a potential defense mechanism adds value and encourages further exploration of countermeasures.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Identity Transformation:** The attacks heavily rely on the ability to craft queries that approximate an identity transformation in GNet.  The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of the limitations imposed by this requirement and the feasibility of applying the attacks to GNets where such transformations are difficult to achieve.
    *   **Limited Defense Strategies:** The proposed defense strategy (API detection) is relatively simple and might be circumvented with more sophisticated attacker strategies. A broader discussion of other potential defenses would enhance the paper.
    *   **Scope of Evaluation:** Although the experimental results are strong, the evaluations mainly concentrate on image-related tasks. Expanding the evaluations to other types of generative networks would demonstrate the broader applicability of the attacks.
    *   **Scalability of Attacks:** The paper demonstrates success with a particular size of image. It's worth considering the limitations in scalability if the underlying victim model uses higher resolution images, and if more computation power is needed for effective watermarking removal.

*   **Impact:** The paper will likely stimulate further research in the following areas:
    * Development of more robust watermarking techniques resistant to query-based reverse engineering.
    * Exploration of different defensive strategies against the attacks outlined in this paper.
    * Analysis of the trade-off between watermark robustness and image quality in box-free watermarking systems.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of deep learning security. It effectively identifies and exploits a vulnerability in a widely used watermarking technique, demonstrating the need for more robust solutions. The experimental results are convincing, and the discussion of potential defenses adds value to the study. Despite some limitations regarding the dependency on identity transformation and the limited scope of the evaluation, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.

Score: 8.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### NWaaS: Nonintrusive Watermarking as a Service for X-to-Image DNN
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18036v1)
- **Authors**: Haonan An, Guang Hua, Yu Guo, Hangcheng Cao, Susanto Rahardja, Yuguang Fang
- **Abstract**: The intellectual property of deep neural network (DNN) models can be protected with DNN watermarking, which embeds copyright watermarks into model parameters (white-box), model behavior (black-box), or model outputs (box-free), and the watermarks can be subsequently extracted to verify model ownership or detect model theft. Despite recent advances, these existing methods are inherently intrusive, as they either modify the model parameters or alter the structure. This natural intrusiveness raises concerns about watermarking-induced shifts in model behavior and the additional cost of fine-tuning, further exacerbated by the rapidly growing model size. As a result, model owners are often reluctant to adopt DNN watermarking in practice, which limits the development of practical Watermarking as a Service (WaaS) systems. To address this issue, we introduce Nonintrusive Watermarking as a Service (NWaaS), a novel trustless paradigm designed for X-to-Image models, in which we hypothesize that with the model untouched, an owner-defined watermark can still be extracted from model outputs. Building on this concept, we propose ShadowMark, a concrete implementation of NWaaS which addresses critical deployment challenges by establishing a robust and nonintrusive side channel in the protected model's black-box API, leveraging a key encoder and a watermark decoder. It is significantly distinctive from existing solutions by attaining the so-called absolute fidelity and being applicable to different DNN architectures, while being also robust against existing attacks, eliminating the fidelity-robustness trade-off. Extensive experiments on image-to-image, noise-to-image, noise-and-text-to-image, and text-to-image models, demonstrate the efficacy and practicality of ShadowMark for real-world deployment of nonintrusive DNN watermarking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Nonintrusive Watermarking as a Service (NWaaS)," a novel approach to watermarking deep neural networks (DNNs), specifically for X-to-Image models.  Unlike existing watermarking techniques that modify model parameters or behavior (white-box, black-box, or box-free), NWaaS aims for "absolute fidelity" by leaving the original model untouched.  The method, implemented as "ShadowMark," establishes a side channel in the model's black-box API.  It uses a key encoder and watermark decoder to embed and extract watermarks from the model's outputs without altering the underlying model. This is achieved by training a separate network to map owner-defined keys to model inputs that produce outputs containing the desired watermark. The paper argues this approach addresses concerns about model degradation, unpredictable behavior changes, and trust issues associated with traditional Watermarking-as-a-Service.  The authors validate ShadowMark through experiments on various X-to-Image models, demonstrating its effectiveness and robustness against attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of non-intrusive watermarking is somewhat novel, especially its realization through a side channel established using a key encoder and watermark decoder in a black-box API. While the individual components (encoders, decoders, black-box attacks) are not entirely new, the combination and application to achieve non-intrusive watermarking is a significant contribution. The concept of perfect fidelity by not modifying the model is a desirable goal.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the practical limitations of existing DNN watermarking techniques (fidelity loss, unpredictable behavior, and trust issues) is highly relevant. The non-intrusive approach could encourage wider adoption of watermarking in commercial settings. The claimed ability to operate in trustless environments further enhances its practicality. The claim of eliminating the fidelity-robustness trade-off, a key issue in existing watermarking methods, is particularly noteworthy.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of existing watermarking methods.
    *   **Novelty in approach:** The non-intrusive nature of NWaaS, especially its implementation as ShadowMark, offers a distinct advantage.
    *   **Practical implications:**  The focus on Watermarking as a Service and trustless environments increases the practical relevance.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The paper provides experimental results across diverse X-to-Image models, demonstrating the feasibility and effectiveness of ShadowMark.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Performance Overhead:** While the model itself is untouched, there will be significant performance and training overhead involved to train the `G` (Key Encoder) and `D` (Watermark Decoder) components to make ShadowMark work. The paper does not discuss this adequately. This is a significant limitation.
    *   **Security Considerations:** The framework hinges upon the key, `k`. If compromised, the watermark could be easily extracted or spoofed. It must have some rigorous key management mechanisms to avoid this single point of failure. The discussion of SRA against brute force provides *some* limited evidence, but this requires more extensive analysis against sophisticated attacks, especially those aimed to learn the relationship between keys and model outputs.
    *   **Dependency on Model Outputs:** The watermark is embedded implicitly into the model output by the additional network. If an adversary can modify the model output before reaching the decoder (e.g., with adversarial perturbations that are imperceptible to the human eye but can confuse the decoder), the watermark extraction can be compromised.
    *   **Generality concerns:** The design focuses heavily on X-to-Image models. The generalizability to other DNN architectures (e.g., classifiers, language models) is unclear and potentially limited without significant modification.
    *   **Stringent Requirements for model parameters:** The paper assumes there is a Key Encoder that takes the model input `x` to create the image `Gy(k)`. Then, there is the Watermark Decoder that takes the model output `Mo(Gy(k))` to generate an output. It must also be that the original input and watermarked input has similar outputs in the original Model that doesn't have the Key Encoder/Watermark Decoder to begin with. These types of requirements can lead to issues in practical uses.

*   **Impact:** If the performance overhead can be significantly reduced and the security concerns adequately addressed, NWaaS could influence the development of more practical and trustworthy DNN watermarking systems. The concept of non-intrusiveness may lead to a new direction for research in this area. However, given the current limitations, its immediate impact is likely to be moderate.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and potentially significant approach to DNN watermarking with some solid strengths. However, the performance and security concerns, the limited generalizability, and stringent requirements on the model parameters require further investigation to realize its full potential. A score of 6 reflects the novelty and potential impact tempered by the identified weaknesses.

**Rigorous Rationale:** The score has been assigned after carefully balancing the paper's innovative ideas and practical limitations. While the concept of non-intrusive watermarking is appealing, the paper does not fully address the trade-offs it introduces, such as the computational overhead and vulnerability to attacks. The experiments are promising but need to be extended to more diverse models and datasets. Therefore, the paper is viewed as a valuable contribution to the field, but it is not yet ready to have a transformative impact due to the limitations identified, justifying the score of 6.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### GrAInS: Gradient-based Attribution for Inference-Time Steering of LLMs and VLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18043v1)
- **Authors**: Duy Nguyen, Archiki Prasad, Elias Stengel-Eskin, Mohit Bansal
- **Abstract**: Inference-time steering methods offer a lightweight alternative to fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) by modifying internal activations at test time without updating model weights. However, most existing approaches rely on fixed, global intervention vectors, overlook the causal influence of individual input tokens, and fail to leverage informative gradients from the model's logits, particularly in multimodal settings where visual and textual inputs contribute unevenly. To address these limitations, we introduce GrAInS, an inference-time steering approach that operates across both language-only and vision-language models and tasks. GrAInS uses contrastive, gradient-based attribution via Integrated Gradients to identify the top-k most influential tokens, both positively and negatively attributed based on their contribution to preferred versus dispreferred outputs. These tokens are then used to construct directional steering vectors that capture semantic shifts from undesirable to desirable behavior. During inference, GrAInS adjusts hidden activations at transformer layers guided by token-level attribution signals, and normalizes activations to preserve representational scale. This enables fine-grained, interpretable, and modular control over model behavior, without retraining or auxiliary supervision. Empirically, GrAInS consistently outperforms both fine-tuning and existing steering baselines: it achieves a 13.22% accuracy gain on TruthfulQA using Llama-3.1-8B, reduces hallucination rates on MMHal-Bench from 0.624 to 0.514 with LLaVA-1.6-7B, and improves alignment win rates on SPA-VL by 8.11%, all while preserving the model's fluency and general capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GRAINS: Gradient-based Attribution for Inference-Time Steering of LLMs and VLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GRAINS, a novel inference-time steering method for both Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs). Unlike existing steering methods that often rely on fixed, global intervention vectors and disregard the causal influence of individual input tokens, GRAINS leverages contrastive, gradient-based attribution (using Integrated Gradients) to identify the most influential tokens.  It constructs directional steering vectors based on the positive and negative contributions of these tokens toward preferred and dispreferred outputs. During inference, GRAINS adjusts hidden activations at transformer layers, guided by token-level attribution signals and normalization to maintain representational scale. The authors demonstrate that GRAINS outperforms fine-tuning and existing steering baselines across various safety-critical tasks such as reducing hallucinations, improving truthfulness, and enhancing alignment, all while preserving general capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper offers a significant improvement over existing inference-time steering methods.  The use of gradient-based attribution to identify influential tokens and construct directional steering vectors is novel.  Existing methods often treat all tokens equally or rely on external object detectors, while GRAINS provides a more nuanced and token-sensitive approach. The integration of positive and negative attributions into the steering process also contributes to its novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the improved performance of GRAINS on safety-critical tasks. Reducing hallucinations, improving truthfulness, and enhancing alignment are crucial for deploying LLMs and VLMs in real-world applications. The fact that GRAINS achieves these improvements without requiring fine-tuning or auxiliary supervision makes it a practical and efficient solution. The method's broad applicability to both LLMs and VLMs further enhances its significance. The preservation of general capabilities, demonstrated through MMLU and MMMU benchmarks, is also an important strength.
    * The integration of interpretability and active intervention is a good contribution that closes the gap between the two.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear and well-motivated solution to a relevant problem.
    *   The method is well-explained and technically sound.
    *   The empirical results are strong, demonstrating significant improvements over existing baselines across multiple datasets and model architectures.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the effectiveness of different components of the method.
    *   The qualitative analysis highlights the practical benefits of GRAINS in correcting specific errors and improving the quality of generated outputs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper provides a good analysis of the impact of hyperparameters like `a` and `k`, more analysis on different model architectures would add value.
    *   Although mentioned, a deeper dive into the computational complexity of the attribution step, especially for larger models and inputs, would be beneficial. (However, they do include runtime)
    *   While the qualitative analysis helps, more in-depth evaluation of the interpretability benefit of being able to understand which tokens were used in steering is important.
    *   The reliance on Integrated Gradients, while justified, might limit the method's applicability in scenarios where calculating gradients is computationally prohibitive.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of inference-time steering. The token-sensitive and attribution-guided approach of GRAINS is a promising direction for future research. The method could be extended to address other types of undesirable behavior, such as bias and toxicity. It could also be integrated with other steering techniques to create more powerful and versatile solutions. Further improvements would be needed to be easily deployed in practice.

*Overall:*
GRAINS makes an important contribution to efficient and flexible LLM/VLM control. The method is theoretically sound, empirically validated and reasonably justified through qualitative evaluations. The novelty and significant results mean it should have a strong influence on the field.

**Score: 8.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Synthetic Data Generation for Phrase Break Prediction with Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18044v1)
- **Authors**: Hoyeon Lee, Sejung Son, Ye-Eun Kang, Jong-Hwan Kim
- **Abstract**: Current approaches to phrase break prediction address crucial prosodic aspects of text-to-speech systems but heavily rely on vast human annotations from audio or text, incurring significant manual effort and cost. Inherent variability in the speech domain, driven by phonetic factors, further complicates acquiring consistent, high-quality data. Recently, large language models (LLMs) have shown success in addressing data challenges in NLP by generating tailored synthetic data while reducing manual annotation needs. Motivated by this, we explore leveraging LLM to generate synthetic phrase break annotations, addressing the challenges of both manual annotation and speech-related tasks by comparing with traditional annotations and assessing effectiveness across multiple languages. Our findings suggest that LLM-based synthetic data generation effectively mitigates data challenges in phrase break prediction and highlights the potential of LLMs as a viable solution for the speech domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate synthetic data for phrase break prediction in text-to-speech (TTS) systems. The authors address the challenges of manual annotation (audio-oriented and text-oriented), which are costly, time-consuming, and prone to inconsistencies. They propose leveraging LLMs to generate synthetic phrase break annotations, comparing them with human-annotated data across English, French, and Spanish. Their findings suggest that LLM-generated data can effectively mitigate data challenges in phrase break prediction, achieving comparable or even superior performance to models trained on human-annotated data, while requiring significantly fewer manual examples. The paper investigates cross-lingual transfer strategies to extend the approach to multiple languages, further reducing annotation costs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of LLMs for synthetic data generation in the *speech domain*, specifically for phrase break prediction, represents a significant step. While LLMs have been used for data augmentation in NLP tasks, their application to tasks involving prosodic features, which are crucial for TTS, is novel. It fills a gap in research where speech-related tasks often have more demanding data requirements but have received less attention in terms of LLM-based data generation. The exploration of cross-lingual transfer learning within this context is also a valuable contribution. However, a major limitation for the overall novelty claim is that LLMs have already been explored for code-switching and code-mixing tasks in text to speech, and the approach the authors use is similar to techniques already explored in these papers.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it addresses a crucial bottleneck in TTS research and development: the need for extensive human annotation. By demonstrating the feasibility of using LLMs to generate high-quality synthetic data, the authors offer a path toward reducing costs, improving consistency, and accelerating the development of phrase break prediction models, particularly in low-resource languages.  The results showing that models trained on synthetic data outperform those trained on human data (specifically in English using the Common Voice dataset) could lead to a paradigm shift in how phrase break prediction data is created.  The cross-lingual experiments demonstrating strong generalizability are especially important for making TTS more accessible to more languages.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges of manual phrase break annotation.
    *   **Rigorous experimentation:**  The paper thoroughly compares different annotation types (audio-oriented, text-oriented, LLM-generated) across multiple languages. The use of pass rate, annotation agreement, Krippendorff's alpha, human score, and F1-score ensures a comprehensive analysis.
    *   **Cross-lingual approach:** The inclusion of French and Spanish, along with an investigation of cross-lingual transfer, strengthens the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Practical validation:**  The use of MiniLM to train models on both human and synthetic data provides a concrete demonstration of the practical value of the generated data.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized, with clear explanations of the methods and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Selection:** The justification for selecting GPT-4o mini could be stronger. Although the paper discusses its performance and cost-effectiveness, a more in-depth comparison with other potentially suitable LLMs would be beneficial. However, given the performance is demonstrated, further LLM selection is possibly redundant.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Details:** While the prompt is shown, a more detailed discussion of the prompt engineering process (e.g., how the few-shot examples were chosen, the iterative refinement of the prompt) would be valuable. This would enable other researchers to replicate and build upon the work.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation primarily focuses on F1-score and human evaluation. Investigating other metrics related to speech naturalness or subjective listening tests could further validate the quality of the synthetic data.
    *   **Generalization to Different Domains**: The study uses specific datasets (news, community websites, and spoken corpora). More investigation into different domains would further ensure applicability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of TTS by demonstrating the feasibility of using LLMs to automate data creation and reduce reliance on expensive and time-consuming human annotation. The cross-lingual capabilities also have the potential to broaden the reach of TTS technology to more languages.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**
The paper presents a novel and significant application of LLMs to the speech domain, addressing a crucial bottleneck in TTS development. The rigorous experimentation and cross-lingual validation strengthen the findings, indicating practical value. However, the limitations regarding prompt engineering detail, the limited scope of evaluation, and the existing prior work in code-switching and code-mixing, particularly in TTS, constrain the novelty and impact to a 7. The paper makes a solid contribution and has the potential to influence future research and development in phrase break prediction and TTS, however, a deeper exploration of the weaknesses would improve the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### RECALLED: An Unbounded Resource Consumption Attack on Large Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18053v1)
- **Authors**: Haoran Gao, Yuanhe Zhang, Zhenhong Zhou, Lei Jiang, Fanyu Meng, Yujia Xiao, Kun Wang, Yang Liu, Junlan Feng
- **Abstract**: Resource Consumption Attacks (RCAs) have emerged as a significant threat to the deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs). With the integration of vision modalities, additional attack vectors exacerbate the risk of RCAs in large vision-language models (LVLMs). However, existing red-teaming studies have largely overlooked visual inputs as a potential attack surface, resulting in insufficient mitigation strategies against RCAs in LVLMs. To address this gap, we propose RECALLED (\textbf{RE}source \textbf{C}onsumption \textbf{A}ttack on \textbf{L}arge Vision-\textbf{L}anguag\textbf{E} Mo\textbf{D}els), the first approach for exploiting visual modalities to trigger unbounded RCAs red-teaming. First, we present \textit{Vision Guided Optimization}, a fine-grained pixel-level optimization, to obtain \textit{Output Recall} adversarial perturbations, which can induce repeating output. Then, we inject the perturbations into visual inputs, triggering unbounded generations to achieve the goal of RCAs. Additionally, we introduce \textit{Multi-Objective Parallel Losses} to generate universal attack templates and resolve optimization conflicts when intending to implement parallel attacks. Empirical results demonstrate that RECALLED increases service response latency by over 26 $\uparrow$, resulting in an additional 20\% increase in GPU utilization and memory consumption. Our study exposes security vulnerabilities in LVLMs and establishes a red-teaming framework that can facilitate future defense development against RCAs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "RECALLED: An Unbounded Resource Consumption Attack on Large Vision-Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RECALLED, a novel method for generating Resource Consumption Attacks (RCAs) specifically targeting Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). It exploits the visual modality to trigger unbounded generation, leading to resource exhaustion. RECALLED employs "Vision Guided Optimization" to craft adversarial perturbations in the visual input, causing repetitive outputs. The authors also propose "Multi-Objective Parallel Loss" to enhance the universality and efficiency of attacks.  Experiments on several LVLMs demonstrate that RECALLED significantly increases service response latency, GPU utilization, and memory consumption, highlighting the vulnerabilities in these models. A defense strategy is proposed that attempts to disrupt the repeating patterns.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its focus on the visual modality as a potent attack vector for RCAs in LVLMs. While text-based RCAs have been studied, the exploration of visual inputs to trigger unbounded generation is a valuable contribution. The Vision Guided Optimization and Multi-Objective Parallel Loss are technically sound approaches, although their individual novelty might be incremental rather than revolutionary. The concept of Output Recall and its instantiation into token-level and sentence-level variants is a well-defined strategy that demonstrably contributes to the attack's effectiveness.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is multifaceted. First, it exposes a real and potentially severe vulnerability in deployed LVLMs. RCAs can significantly degrade service availability and reliability, impacting user experience and potentially leading to financial losses. Second, it establishes a red-teaming framework for evaluating the robustness of LVLMs against visual-based attacks. This is crucial for developing effective defenses. The experimental results are comprehensive and convincing, demonstrating the effectiveness of RECALLED and the vulnerabilities of state-of-the-art LVLMs. The proposal of even a basic defense mechanism shows that they are thinking about the practical impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the gap in existing research regarding RCAs on LVLMs through visual inputs.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** RECALLED is a well-defined and technically sound method with clear steps and components.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are conducted on multiple state-of-the-art LVLMs and thoroughly evaluate the effectiveness of RECALLED.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The paper provides insights into the attack generation mechanisms and demonstrates the limitations of existing defenses.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Defense Mechanism:** The proposed defense mechanism, while a step in the right direction, appears to be relatively basic and potentially disruptive to legitimate queries.  It primarily addresses the *symptoms* of the attack (repetition) rather than the root cause (the vulnerability in the model's processing of specific visual patterns). A more sophisticated defense mechanism would significantly strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Scope:** While covering multiple models, a deeper exploration of *why* these specific models are vulnerable (beyond just their general architecture) could be insightful. What specifically causes these models to fall into the pattern generation loop?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by:

    *   Raising awareness about the security risks associated with visual inputs in LVLMs.
    *   Motivating further research on robust defenses against RCAs in LVLMs.
    *   Encouraging the development of red-teaming frameworks for evaluating the security and reliability of LVLMs.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:**
    The paper makes a solid contribution by exploring a previously underexplored attack vector on LVLMs and providing a relatively simple methodology to achieve it. The experimental evaluation is thorough, and while the countermeasure is a bit basic, it shows potential future directions. The core ideas are well defined and demonstrated and could have some broader influence. Thus, a score of 7 reflects this combination of novelty, significance, and potential for future impact. While not groundbreaking, it represents a valuable and well-executed contribution to the field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Privacy-Preserving Synthetic Review Generation with Diverse Writing Styles Using LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18055v1)
- **Authors**: Tevin Atwal, Chan Nam Tieu, Yefeng Yuan, Zhan Shi, Yuhong Liu, Liang Cheng
- **Abstract**: The increasing use of synthetic data generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) presents both opportunities and challenges in data-driven applications. While synthetic data provides a cost-effective, scalable alternative to real-world data to facilitate model training, its diversity and privacy risks remain underexplored. Focusing on text-based synthetic data, we propose a comprehensive set of metrics to quantitatively assess the diversity (i.e., linguistic expression, sentiment, and user perspective), and privacy (i.e., re-identification risk and stylistic outliers) of synthetic datasets generated by several state-of-the-art LLMs. Experiment results reveal significant limitations in LLMs' capabilities in generating diverse and privacy-preserving synthetic data. Guided by the evaluation results, a prompt-based approach is proposed to enhance the diversity of synthetic reviews while preserving reviewer privacy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of generating diverse and privacy-preserving synthetic review data using Large Language Models (LLMs).  It proposes a comprehensive framework to quantitatively evaluate the diversity (lexical richness, semantic variation, sentiment) and privacy risks (re-identification and stylistic outliers) of synthetic reviews. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art LLMs (GPT-4 and Claude 3.7 Sonnet).  Guided by the evaluation results, they introduce a prompt-based approach with automatic prompt optimization to enhance the diversity of synthetic reviews while preserving user privacy. The dynamic prompt optimization refines instructions based on metric failures, leading to more semantically rich and stylistically varied, privacy-compliant reviews.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Framework:** The paper's strength lies in its detailed and well-defined set of metrics for evaluating both diversity and privacy in synthetic text data. Quantifying these aspects is crucial for responsible use of LLMs in data generation.
    *   **Practical Problem and Solution:** Generating realistic and privacy-respecting synthetic data is a significant and timely problem, especially given increasing data scarcity and privacy regulations. The proposed prompt optimization technique offers a pragmatic solution.
    *   **Iterative Prompt Optimization:** The dynamic prompt adjustment mechanism addresses the weaknesses of static prompts, allowing for the iterative refinement of LLM output. This approach can lead to more nuanced and balanced synthetic datasets.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper includes empirical evaluations on both real-world and synthetic datasets, providing evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed framework and techniques.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty in Metrics:** While the framework is comprehensive, many of the metrics used are adapted from existing literature (N-grams, semantic diversity, sentiment analysis). The novelty mainly stems from combining them in a specific application context.
    *   **Dependency on Prompt Engineering:** The success of the prompt optimization technique heavily relies on the design and quality of the initial base prompt and the pool of refinement prompts. The paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion on how these prompts are constructed.
    *   **Scalability of User Profiling:** The stylistic outlier detection based on user profiling can be computationally expensive, especially for large datasets with numerous users. The paper acknowledges some of these issues but doesn't offer a truly scalable solution.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Trade-offs:** While the paper acknowledges trade-offs between diversity and privacy, it could delve deeper into specific strategies for balancing these competing objectives.
    *   **Generalization to Other Domains:** The paper is specific to product review data. While the framework could be adapted, the applicability and effectiveness of the proposed techniques in other domains (e.g., medical records, financial data) are not explored.

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper lies mainly in the *application* of a comprehensive evaluation framework and iterative prompt optimization to the *specific problem* of generating privacy-preserving and diverse synthetic *review data*. While individual metrics and prompting techniques are known, the *integrated system* and its application to review generation represent a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the emerging field of synthetic data generation using LLMs. It provides a practical framework for evaluating and improving the quality and privacy of synthetic text data.  The prompt optimization technique offers a tangible approach for mitigating the limitations of LLMs in generating realistic and diverse synthetic data.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by providing a benchmark for evaluating synthetic review data generation methods. It can encourage more systematic and quantitative evaluations of diversity and privacy risks. The iterative prompt optimization technique could be adopted and extended by other researchers working in synthetic data generation.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid, well-executed study that addresses a relevant problem. The comprehensive evaluation framework and the prompt optimization technique are valuable contributions. However, the limited novelty in the metrics and the reliance on prompt engineering prevent it from achieving a higher score. The potential influence is moderate, as the paper provides a specific solution within a particular domain, but the principles and techniques could be adapted to other contexts. The paper is a strong contribution but doesn't represent a groundbreaking advancement.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### BokehDiff: Neural Lens Blur with One-Step Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18060v1)
- **Authors**: Chengxuan Zhu, Qingnan Fan, Qi Zhang, Jinwei Chen, Huaqi Zhang, Chao Xu, Boxin Shi
- **Abstract**: We introduce BokehDiff, a novel lens blur rendering method that achieves physically accurate and visually appealing outcomes, with the help of generative diffusion prior. Previous methods are bounded by the accuracy of depth estimation, generating artifacts in depth discontinuities. Our method employs a physics-inspired self-attention module that aligns with the image formation process, incorporating depth-dependent circle of confusion constraint and self-occlusion effects. We adapt the diffusion model to the one-step inference scheme without introducing additional noise, and achieve results of high quality and fidelity. To address the lack of scalable paired data, we propose to synthesize photorealistic foregrounds with transparency with diffusion models, balancing authenticity and scene diversity.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** BokehDiff presents a new method for rendering lens blur effects that combines physical accuracy with visual quality using generative diffusion models. Unlike earlier approaches that struggled with depth estimation inaccuracies leading to artifacts, BokehDiff incorporates a self-attention module that respects the image formation process, focusing on depth-dependent factors like circle of confusion and self-occlusion. The paper further innovates by utilizing a one-step inference method within the diffusion model, avoiding extra noise while achieving high-fidelity results. To overcome the challenge of limited paired data for training, the authors propose synthesizing photorealistic foregrounds with transparency through diffusion models, achieving a balance between realism and scene variation. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of BokehDiff lies in its approach to rendering lens blurs, as it effectively integrates generative diffusion with physical principles to enhance visual outcomes, particularly in handling artifacts associated with depth transitions. The use of a self-attention mechanism tailored to the image formation process denotes an important advancement over prior techniques that may have overlooked these aspects. However, while the methodological improvements are notable, several factors can temper the perceived significance of the paper. Firstly, diffusion models are not new and have been exploited in various imaging contexts, which may dilute the novelty of the specific application presented here. Additionally, the novelty could be diminished by the reliance on existing depth estimation techniques, which still face limitations in real-world applications. Although synthesizing photorealistic foregrounds is an interesting contribution, the humor in balancing authenticity and diversity raises concerns about overfitting to particular scene characteristics, potentially affecting the generalizability of the method. In conclusion, BokehDiff provides meaningful improvements in lens blur rendering with practical implications, particularly in enhancing visual fidelity and addressing limitations found in traditional methods. However, the approach's reliance on established techniques and the potential limitations of its synthetic data raise questions about its broader applicability and transformative potential in the field. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TELEVAL: A Dynamic Benchmark Designed for Spoken Language Models in Chinese Interactive Scenarios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18061v1)
- **Authors**: Zehan Li, Hongjie Chen, Yuxin Zhang, Jing Zhou, Xuening Wang, Hang Lv, Mengjie Du, Yaodong Song, Jie Lian, Jian Kang, Jie Li, Yongxiang Li, Zhongjiang He, Xuelong Li
- **Abstract**: Spoken language models (SLMs) have seen rapid progress in recent years, along with the development of numerous benchmarks for evaluating their performance. However, most existing benchmarks primarily focus on evaluating whether SLMs can perform complex tasks comparable to those tackled by large language models (LLMs), often failing to align with how users naturally interact in real-world conversational scenarios. In this paper, we propose TELEVAL, a dynamic benchmark specifically designed to evaluate SLMs' effectiveness as conversational agents in realistic Chinese interactive settings. TELEVAL defines three evaluation dimensions: Explicit Semantics, Paralinguistic and Implicit Semantics, and System Abilities. It adopts a dialogue format consistent with real-world usage and evaluates text and audio outputs separately. TELEVAL particularly focuses on the model's ability to extract implicit cues from user speech and respond appropriately without additional instructions. Our experiments demonstrate that despite recent progress, existing SLMs still have considerable room for improvement in natural conversational tasks. We hope that TELEVAL can serve as a user-centered evaluation framework that directly reflects the user experience and contributes to the development of more capable dialogue-oriented SLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TELEVAL: A Dynamic Benchmark Designed for Spoken Language Models in Chinese Interactive Scenarios":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TELEVAL, a new benchmark specifically designed to evaluate Spoken Language Models (SLMs) in realistic Chinese interactive scenarios.  The authors argue that existing benchmarks often focus on complex tasks comparable to Large Language Models (LLMs) but fail to adequately assess how well SLMs perform in natural, real-world conversational settings. TELEVAL aims to address this gap by defining three evaluation dimensions: Explicit Semantics, Paralinguistic and Implicit Semantics, and System Abilities. It adopts a dialogue format, assesses both text and audio outputs, and importantly, focuses on a model's ability to extract implicit cues from user speech and respond appropriately without explicit instructions.  The paper presents initial experimental results demonstrating that current SLMs still have room for improvement in such natural conversational tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a real gap in the evaluation of SLMs.  Existing benchmarks often emphasize task completion (e.g., answering questions, solving math problems) at the expense of natural interaction, pragmatic understanding, and sensitivity to paralinguistic cues (e.g., tone of voice, emotion). Creating a benchmark explicitly designed to evaluate these aspects in the Chinese language is a valuable contribution. The focus on implicit semantics and the ability to respond appropriately without explicit instructions is a particularly novel element. The dynamic and extensible nature of TELEVAL is also a strength.

*   **Significance:**  If TELEVAL gains adoption, it could significantly influence the direction of SLM research.  By providing a benchmark that prioritizes naturalness and user experience, it can encourage researchers to develop SLMs that are not just accurate, but also engaging, empathetic, and adaptable to real-world conversational contexts.  The creation of a comprehensive evaluation pipeline with speech/text inputs and outputs supporting both inference and evaluation for SLMs and LLMs has the potential to push the field further. The design for Chinese interaction scenarios will facilitate progress in that specific setting.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined evaluation dimensions:** The three dimensions (Explicit Semantics, Paralinguistic and Implicit Semantics, and System Abilities) provide a clear and structured framework for evaluation.
    *   **Emphasis on realistic scenarios:** The adoption of a dialogue format and the focus on implicit cues make the benchmark more representative of real-world user interactions.
    *   **Separate evaluation of text and audio:** This helps to isolate the strengths and weaknesses of the SLM's audio understanding and generation components. The dual strategy allows for better diagnosing the source of potential problems.
    *   **Rigorous data construction methodology:** The paper describes a detailed process for creating the TELEVAL datasets, including the use of both synthesized and real human recordings.
    *   **Clear Evaluation Metrics:** The paper makes use of string matching, LLM-based judging, and several evaluation metrics.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Experimental Results:** While the paper presents initial experimental results, they are somewhat high-level.  More detailed analysis of the performance of different SLMs on specific subtasks would strengthen the paper. It would be useful to examine error modes, common failures, and qualitative examples of successful and unsuccessful interactions.
    *   **Reliance on LLM-as-Judge:** While the authors acknowledge the potential biases of LLM-based evaluation and attempt to mitigate them through careful prompt design and evaluation procedures, this remains a potential limitation.  The subjectivity inherent in LLM-based judgment could introduce variability into the benchmark results. Furthermore, it would be useful to benchmark LLM-as-judge evaluations with human evaluations.
    *   **Chinese-Specific:** While specializing in Chinese interactions is beneficial, the lack of cross-lingual evaluations is a drawback.
    *   **Relatively small set of models evaluated:** The experiments are on a small set of 8 SLMs, and one API call to the Google. It would benefit from a broader set.

*   **Potential Influence:** TELEVAL has the potential to become a widely used benchmark for SLMs in Chinese interactive scenarios, leading to more capable and user-friendly conversational agents. As the authors mention and future releases expand, the influence and impact will expand greatly.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5**. The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of SLM evaluation. The development of a new benchmark tailored to Chinese interactive scenarios, with a strong focus on naturalness and implicit semantics, addresses a real gap in the existing literature. While the current experimental results are somewhat limited, the well-defined evaluation framework and the detailed data construction methodology demonstrate the potential value of TELEVAL. The reliance on LLM-as-judge is a limitation, but the authors' efforts to mitigate bias are commendable. Overall, the TELEVAL benchmark has the potential to significantly influence the direction of SLM research in Chinese interactive scenarios and lead to the development of more capable and user-friendly conversational agents. The potential for influence on the field combined with limited experimental results and reliance on LLM-based judgement results in this rating.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Group Sequence Policy Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18071v1)
- **Authors**: Chujie Zheng, Shixuan Liu, Mingze Li, Xiong-Hui Chen, Bowen Yu, Chang Gao, Kai Dang, Yuqiong Liu, Rui Men, An Yang, Jingren Zhou, Junyang Lin
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), our stable, efficient, and performant reinforcement learning algorithm for training large language models. Unlike previous algorithms that adopt token-level importance ratios, GSPO defines the importance ratio based on sequence likelihood and performs sequence-level clipping, rewarding, and optimization. We demonstrate that GSPO achieves superior training efficiency and performance compared to the GRPO algorithm, notably stabilizes Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) RL training, and has the potential for simplifying the design of RL infrastructure. These merits of GSPO have contributed to the remarkable improvements in the latest Qwen3 models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), a new reinforcement learning (RL) algorithm designed for training large language models (LLMs). GSPO addresses instability issues observed in prior RL methods, specifically GRPO, by defining importance ratios based on sequence likelihood rather than token-level likelihoods. The algorithm performs sequence-level clipping, rewarding, and optimization. The authors claim that GSPO achieves superior training stability, efficiency, and performance compared to GRPO, particularly in the context of training Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models.  They demonstrate that GSPO obviates the need for specialized stabilization strategies like Routing Replay, simplifying the RL infrastructure. The paper claims GSPO has contributed to the improvements in the latest Qwen3 models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in shifting the focus from token-level to sequence-level importance weighting within the RL framework for LLMs. While importance sampling itself is not new, its application at the sequence level in this context, coupled with sequence-level clipping, represents a potentially significant adaptation.  The GSPO-token variant also provides some flexibility.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing reliance on RL for fine-tuning LLMs. Stability is a known challenge in this area, and the paper attempts to address it by proposing a more stable and efficient RL algorithm. The claim that GSPO simplifies infrastructure by removing the necessity for Routing Replay is significant, as it could reduce complexity and resource requirements.  The successful deployment of GSPO in training Qwen3 models is an important indicator of practical value.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem with token-level importance weighting in GRPO.
    *   GSPO's objective function and its theoretical connection to sequence likelihood are well-defined.
    *   The empirical results, although specific to the Qwen models, demonstrate tangible improvements in training stability and efficiency.
    *   The analysis of clipping fractions and their impact on training efficiency provides useful insights.
    *   The discussion of GSPO's benefits for MoE training and RL infrastructure highlights its potential broader impact.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper could benefit from more extensive experimentation on a wider range of LLMs and tasks. The focus on the Qwen models, while understandable given the authors' affiliation, limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   While the theoretical rationale is present, more formal analysis of the stability properties of GSPO compared to GRPO would strengthen the paper.
    *   The paper does not provide a detailed ablation study showing the individual contribution of each component (e.g., sequence-level weighting vs. sequence-level clipping).
    *   The algorithm seems highly tailored to language models, and its potential applicability in other domains remains unclear.
    *   While the benefits for RL infrastructure are mentioned, a more detailed comparison of the resources required by GSPO compared to GRPO would be valuable. The claim regarding simplification requires more quantification.

*   **Justification for the Score:**

    The paper presents a novel and potentially significant improvement to RL fine-tuning for LLMs. The shift to sequence-level importance weighting and clipping appears to address a key instability issue. However, the limited empirical evaluation and lack of broader theoretical analysis prevent a higher score. The practical deployment on Qwen3 models is a key strength. Given the promising results, but limited scope and analysis, a score of 7 is appropriate.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Squeeze10-LLM: Squeezing LLMs' Weights by 10 Times via a Staged Mixed-Precision Quantization Method
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18073v1)
- **Authors**: Qingcheng Zhu, Yangyang Ren, Linlin Yang, Mingbao Lin, Yanjing Li, Sheng Xu, Zichao Feng, Haodong Zhu, Yuguang Yang, Juan Zhang, Runqi Wang, Baochang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Deploying large language models (LLMs) is challenging due to their massive parameters and high computational costs. Ultra low-bit quantization can significantly reduce storage and accelerate inference, but extreme compression (i.e., mean bit-width <= 2) often leads to severe performance degradation. To address this, we propose Squeeze10-LLM, effectively "squeezing" 16-bit LLMs' weights by 10 times. Specifically, Squeeze10-LLM is a staged mixed-precision post-training quantization (PTQ) framework and achieves an average of 1.6 bits per weight by quantizing 80% of the weights to 1 bit and 20% to 4 bits. We introduce Squeeze10LLM with two key innovations: Post-Binarization Activation Robustness (PBAR) and Full Information Activation Supervision (FIAS). PBAR is a refined weight significance metric that accounts for the impact of quantization on activations, improving accuracy in low-bit settings. FIAS is a strategy that preserves full activation information during quantization to mitigate cumulative error propagation across layers. Experiments on LLaMA and LLaMA2 show that Squeeze10-LLM achieves state-of-the-art performance for sub-2bit weight-only quantization, improving average accuracy from 43% to 56% on six zero-shot classification tasks--a significant boost over existing PTQ methods. Our code will be released upon publication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Squeeze10-LLM: Squeezing LLMs' Weights by 10 Times via a Staged Mixed-Precision Quantization Method" presents a novel post-training quantization (PTQ) framework designed to compress large language models (LLMs) with minimal performance degradation. The method, Squeeze10-LLM, achieves an average of 1.6 bits per weight by quantizing 80% of the weights to 1 bit and the remaining 20% to 4 bits. The key innovations are Post-Binarization Activation Robustness (PBAR), a refined weight significance metric accounting for the impact of quantization on activations, and Full Information Activation Supervision (FIAS), which preserves full activation information during quantization to mitigate error propagation across layers.  Experiments on LLaMA and LLaMA2 show the method achieves state-of-the-art performance for sub-2bit weight-only quantization, significantly improving accuracy compared to existing PTQ methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel combination of techniques for ultra-low-bit quantization.  The PBAR metric is a clear advance over existing salience metrics by considering the impact of binarization on activations, directly addressing a weakness in previous approaches. The FIAS strategy is also novel in its emphasis on maintaining stable activations during quantization to reduce cumulative errors, a particularly important aspect in ultra-low-bit scenarios. The staged quantization approach itself is not entirely new, but the specific combination with PBAR and FIAS, and the focus on aggressive compression (10x) is a distinctive aspect of this work.

*   **Significance:** Quantization is a critical area for enabling the deployment of LLMs on resource-constrained devices. The paper's ability to achieve a 10x compression with relatively low performance degradation has significant practical implications.  The substantial accuracy improvement over existing sub-2bit quantization methods is a strong indicator of its value. Successfully implementing this approach can broaden the accessibility of LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined technical contributions (PBAR and FIAS).
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance compared to existing methods.
    *   Ablation studies that dissect the contribution of individual components (PBAR and FIAS).
    *   Extensive experiments across multiple models (LLaMA, LLaMA2) and datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper compares to other methods well, it would be beneficial to see more analysis of the *types* of errors that are corrected by PBAR and FIAS. For instance, do they primarily improve performance on specific tasks, or for specific layers in the network? Understanding *where* the benefits are largest could further refine the approach.
    *   The explanation of why 4-bit intermediate quantization is optimal is supported by experimental results (Figure 4, Table 3) but could benefit from a more in-depth theoretical explanation.
    *   The reliance on the Hessian might present scalability issues for larger models, as Hessian computation can be computationally expensive. The paper could address this limitation more explicitly, perhaps suggesting strategies for approximate Hessian computation.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM quantization, paving the way for more efficient deployment of these models. The proposed techniques are likely to be adopted and further developed by other researchers working in this area.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:** The paper presents a solid contribution with novel and technically sound methods. While there are minor limitations (discussed above), the experimental results are compelling, demonstrating clear improvements over existing approaches. The techniques (PBAR, FIAS) are well-motivated and designed. Therefore, the paper is a worthwhile contribution to the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Hybrid and Unitary Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models: Methods and Benchmarking under Resource Constraints
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18076v1)
- **Authors**: Haomin Qi, Zihan Dai, Chengbo Huang
- **Abstract**: Fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) remains a computational bottleneck due to their scale and memory demands. This paper presents a comprehensive evaluation of parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) techniques, including LoRA, BOFT, LoRA-GA, and uRNN, and introduces a novel hybrid strategy that dynamically integrates BOFT's orthogonal stability with LoRA-GA's gradient-aligned rapid convergence. By computing per-layer adaptive updates guided by gradient norms, the hybrid method achieves superior convergence efficiency and generalization across diverse tasks. We also explore, for the first time, the adaptation of unitary RNN (uRNN) principles to transformer-based LLMs, enhancing gradient stability through structured unitary constraints. Empirical evaluations on four benchmarks -- GLUE, GSM8K, MT-Bench, and HumanEval -- using models ranging from 7B to 405B parameters demonstrate that our hybrid method consistently outperforms individual PEFT baselines, approaching full fine-tuning accuracy while reducing resource consumption by up to 2.1 times in training time and 50 percent in memory usage. These findings establish the hybrid approach as a practical and scalable fine-tuning solution for real-world deployment of LLMs under resource constraints.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the computational bottleneck of fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) by proposing a novel hybrid fine-tuning approach. This hybrid method dynamically integrates LoRA-GA's gradient-aligned updates with BOFT's orthogonal transformations at a per-layer level, guided by gradient norms.  It also explores adapting unitary RNN (uRNN) principles to transformer-based LLMs to enhance gradient stability. The authors empirically evaluate their approach and other PEFT baselines on four benchmarks (GLUE, GSM8K, MT-Bench, and HumanEval) using models ranging from 7B to 405B parameters. Their results show the hybrid method consistently outperforms individual PEFT baselines, approaching full fine-tuning accuracy while reducing training time and memory usage. The paper claims the approach is practical and scalable for real-world deployment under resource constraints.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents two main contributions: a hybrid LoRA-GA/BOFT fine-tuning approach and the application of uRNN principles to transformers.

    *   **Hybrid Approach:** The novelty of combining LoRA-GA and BOFT with dynamic weighting based on gradient norms is reasonable. While existing works have explored individual PEFT techniques, the proposed dynamic fusion strategy adds a layer of adaptivity that potentially addresses the weaknesses of each individual method. However, it's an incremental improvement over existing PEFT methods and not a revolutionary architectural innovation.

    *   **uRNN adaptation to Transformers:** Applying uRNN concepts to transformers is interesting. However, given the differences in architectural design between RNNs and transformers (specifically, transformers have self-attention), the direct adaptation may not fully leverage the potential benefits of unitary matrices in transformers. More justification could have been added in this choice and its impact.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Practicality:** The results suggest that the hybrid approach offers a practical solution for fine-tuning LLMs under resource constraints. The demonstrated reduction in training time and memory usage could make LLMs more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited computational resources.

    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper conducts a comprehensive evaluation across a range of benchmarks and model sizes. This strengthens the claim that the hybrid approach is effective and scalable. The comparison to multiple baselines is also valuable.

    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper lacks crucial information that would enable reproducibility of the experiments. The exact hyperparameter settings and details of the uRNN integration within Transformer blocks are not readily available.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** The paper would benefit from a more detailed ablation study to understand the contribution of each component (LoRA-GA and BOFT) in the hybrid approach. It's unclear whether the performance gains are solely due to the combination or if one component dominates the results.

    *   **Justification for Design Choices:** The paper doesn't fully justify some design choices, such as the specific way uRNN principles are incorporated into transformers. A more detailed explanation of the rationale behind these choices would improve the paper's clarity and impact.

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:**  The paper mentions hyperparameter tuning but doesn't provide enough details about the tuning process. The performance of PEFT methods can be highly sensitive to hyperparameters, so it's important to provide more information about how the optimal settings were found.
    *   **Presentation:**  The paper could be strengthened by making the experimental setup and contributions of each method more readable. The experimental details are hard to pull out from the rest of the paper.
*   **Potential Influence:** The hybrid approach has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and scalable fine-tuning techniques for LLMs. The idea of dynamically combining different PEFT strategies could inspire future research in this area. However, its novelty is incremental and the paper relies heavily on empirical results without digging into the deeper reasons why the results are working and how it can be improved.

**Score:** 6

**Rationale:** The paper presents a practically useful hybrid fine-tuning approach for LLMs with a thorough empirical evaluation. The concept of dynamically fusing LoRA-GA and BOFT is novel, although it can be considered an incremental advancement. The introduction of uRNN principles to transformers is an interesting attempt, but it warrants a deeper investigation and justification. The limited ablation study, lack of detailed hyperparameter settings, and unclear design choices weaken the paper's impact. Overall, it is a decent contribution to the field of PEFT, but it lacks the depth and significant novelty to warrant a higher score. The experiments are comprehensive and validate the proposed idea. It is worth future explorations, but the current contributions are incremental.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Understanding the Supply Chain and Risks of Large Language Model Applications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18105v1)
- **Authors**: Yujie Ma, Lili Quan, Xiaofei Xie, Qiang Hu, Jiongchi Yu, Yao Zhang, Sen Chen
- **Abstract**: The rise of Large Language Models (LLMs) has led to the widespread deployment of LLM-based systems across diverse domains. As these systems proliferate, understanding the risks associated with their complex supply chains is increasingly important. LLM-based systems are not standalone as they rely on interconnected supply chains involving pretrained models, third-party libraries, datasets, and infrastructure. Yet, most risk assessments narrowly focus on model or data level, overlooking broader supply chain vulnerabilities. While recent studies have begun to address LLM supply chain risks, there remains a lack of benchmarks for systematic research. To address this gap, we introduce the first comprehensive dataset for analyzing and benchmarking LLM supply chain security. We collect 3,859 real-world LLM applications and perform interdependency analysis, identifying 109,211 models, 2,474 datasets, and 9,862 libraries. We extract model fine-tuning paths, dataset reuse, and library reliance, mapping the ecosystem's structure. To evaluate security, we gather 1,555 risk-related issues-50 for applications, 325 for models, 18 for datasets, and 1,229 for libraries from public vulnerability databases. Using this dataset, we empirically analyze component dependencies and risks. Our findings reveal deeply nested dependencies in LLM applications and significant vulnerabilities across the supply chain, underscoring the need for comprehensive security analysis. We conclude with practical recommendations to guide researchers and developers toward safer, more trustworthy LLM-enabled systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Understanding the Supply Chain and Risks of Large Language Model Applications":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLMSCBench, a novel dataset designed to facilitate the analysis and benchmarking of security risks within the supply chains of Large Language Model (LLM) applications. The dataset comprises real-world LLM applications, their dependencies (models, datasets, and libraries), and associated risk issues. The authors perform an inter-dependency analysis to map these components and then analyze the security posture of the LLM ecosystem using vulnerability databases and user-reported issues. The paper provides an empirical study using LLMSCBench to uncover dependencies and risks, revealing complex, deeply nested structures and significant vulnerabilities across the LLM supply chain. The authors conclude with practical implications and actionable recommendations for researchers and developers, emphasizing the need for robust security and privacy practices.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation and public release of LLMSCBench.
