While previous work has discussed the risks of LLM supply chains at a conceptual level, this paper takes a significant step towards creating a concrete, usable resource for empirical analysis.  The inter-dependency analysis is also a notable contribution, as it allows for a more granular understanding of how vulnerabilities can propagate through the ecosystem. Existing works like Wang et al. (2025a) and Huang et al. (2024) have identified potential risks, but LLMSCBench provides a benchmark for systematically evaluating these risks. The combination of application data, model details, dataset information, and library dependencies in a single dataset is a significant step forward.

**Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the increasing importance of LLMs in real-world applications and the corresponding need to address their security vulnerabilities. By providing a benchmark dataset, the authors enable further research into:

*   Risk propagation within LLM supply chains.
*   Developing automated tools for vulnerability detection and mitigation.
*   Establishing best practices for secure LLM application development.

The paper's findings on the prevalence of vulnerabilities and complex dependencies highlight the urgency of these efforts. The detailed analysis of specific risk types and the identification of key vulnerabilities in commonly used libraries are valuable contributions. The findings pointing to the critical need for systematic tracking of risks in models and datasets like current CVE system are also of great significance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Dataset:** LLMSCBench is a large and comprehensive dataset that addresses a significant gap in the field.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough analysis of the dataset, uncovering valuable insights into the structure and risks of LLM supply chains.
*   **Actionable Recommendations:** The authors offer concrete recommendations for researchers, developers, and policymakers, providing a roadmap for future work.
*   **Well-written and organized:** The paper is clearly written and well-structured, making it accessible to a broad audience.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Data Source Limitations:** The dataset is limited to LLM applications available on Hugging Face. Although Hugging Face is a popular platform, it may not be fully representative of the entire LLM ecosystem.
*   **Risk Identification Subjectivity:** The manual extraction and categorization of risk issues from user discussions is a subjective process, potentially introducing bias.  The authors address this with multi-person review and consensus, but the inherent subjectivity remains a limitation.
*   **Limited Dataset Relationships:** The paper acknowledges challenges in fully capturing dataset relationships, which could lead to incomplete dependency chains.
*   **Static Analysis Focus:** The analysis primarily relies on static code analysis and vulnerability databases, lacking in-depth runtime or dynamic vulnerability assessments. Future analyses could incorporate more active security testing.

**Justification for Score:**

While LLMSCBench is a valuable contribution and addresses a crucial need, it is essential to recognize its limitations. The dependency on a single platform (Hugging Face), the inherent subjectivity in risk issue extraction, the limited dataset relationships, and the static analysis focus represent tangible constraints. However, the dataset's comprehensive nature and the paper's thorough analysis make it a significant step forward. This contribution paves the way for future research.

Score: 7.5

**Rationale:** This score reflects the paper's substantial contribution in providing a novel and useful dataset, enabling empirical research on a pressing problem. The detailed analysis and actionable recommendations further enhance its value. However, the limitations related to data source, subjectivity, data relationships, and analytical depth prevent it from achieving a higher score. Future work that addresses these limitations could significantly increase the impact and significance of this research area.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of 3D DDPM for MRI Image Generation Using Tensor Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18112v1)
- **Authors**: Binghua Li, Ziqing Chang, Tong Liang, Chao Li, Toshihisa Tanaka, Shigeki Aoki, Qibin Zhao, Zhe Sun
- **Abstract**: We address the challenge of parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) for three-dimensional (3D) U-Net-based denoising diffusion probabilistic models (DDPMs) in magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) image generation. Despite its practical significance, research on parameter-efficient representations of 3D convolution operations remains limited. To bridge this gap, we propose Tensor Volumetric Operator (TenVOO), a novel PEFT method specifically designed for fine-tuning DDPMs with 3D convolutional backbones. Leveraging tensor network modeling, TenVOO represents 3D convolution kernels with lower-dimensional tensors, effectively capturing complex spatial dependencies during fine-tuning with few parameters. We evaluate TenVOO on three downstream brain MRI datasets-ADNI, PPMI, and BraTS2021-by fine-tuning a DDPM pretrained on 59,830 T1-weighted brain MRI scans from the UK Biobank. Our results demonstrate that TenVOO achieves state-of-the-art performance in multi-scale structural similarity index measure (MS-SSIM), outperforming existing approaches in capturing spatial dependencies while requiring only 0.3% of the trainable parameters of the original model. Our code is available at: https://github.com/xiaovhua/tenvoo
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Tensor Volumetric Operator (TenVOO), a parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) method specifically designed for 3D U-Net-based denoising diffusion probabilistic models (DDPMs) used in magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) image generation. TenVOO leverages tensor network modeling to represent 3D convolution kernels with lower-dimensional tensors, aiming to capture complex spatial dependencies with fewer parameters. The method is evaluated on three brain MRI datasets (ADNI, PPMI, and BraTS2021) by fine-tuning a DDPM pretrained on a large UK Biobank MRI dataset. The results demonstrate that TenVOO achieves competitive performance (measured by MS-SSIM, FID, and MMD) while using a significantly smaller number of trainable parameters compared to full fine-tuning and other PEFT methods like LoRA, LoKr, and LoHa. The paper also explores variants of TenVOO (TenVOO-L and TenVOO-Q) and investigates the impact of tensor rank on performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of tensor networks for parameter-efficient fine-tuning of 3D convolutional layers in DDPMs for MRI image generation constitutes a novel contribution. The paper identifies a practical need (efficient adaptation of 3D models in medical imaging) and proposes a tailored solution (TenVOO). While tensor networks themselves are not new, their adaptation and application in this specific context, especially with the TenVOO-L and TenVOO-Q variants, demonstrate innovation.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to reduce the computational cost and data requirements for adapting large 3D generative models to specific MRI tasks. This has implications for wider adoption in clinical settings where computational resources or specific disease datasets might be limited. Demonstrating superior or comparable performance to existing PEFT methods with a fraction of the parameters is a valuable contribution. The successful application across multiple MRI datasets (ADNI, PPMI, BraTS2021), fine-tuning from a large pre-trained model enhances the significance of the results.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of fine-tuning large 3D models for MRI and the need for parameter-efficient methods.
    *   **Technical Contribution:** TenVOO is well-explained and motivated by the desire to efficiently capture spatial dependencies in MRI data. The comparison between TenVOO-L and TenVOO-Q, drawing inspiration from LoRA and QuanTA, strengthens the technical contribution.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on multiple datasets with appropriate evaluation metrics (FID, MMD, MS-SSIM). The performance gains in terms of MS-SSIM indicate that TenVOO effectively captures spatial dependencies. The analysis of tensor rank contributes to a deeper understanding of the method's behavior.
    *   **Code Availability:** Open-source code enhances reproducibility and encourages further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper includes an ablation study on the rank, a more in-depth analysis of different architectural choices within TenVOO (e.g., the specific tensorization schemes) would be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of Direct Comparison with SOTA generative methods** It would be useful to directly compare the results against other specialized state-of-the-art generative methods for brain MRI synthesis rather than just PEFT methods.
    *   **Limited Focus on Clinical Utility:** While the paper demonstrates improved performance on metrics, it lacks a detailed discussion of the potential clinical applications and benefits of using TenVOO for MRI image generation.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence research in parameter-efficient fine-tuning for 3D medical imaging. It provides a valuable approach for adapting large models to specific clinical tasks with limited resources. The code availability is also likely to encourage adoption and further development.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to parameter-efficient fine-tuning for 3D medical imaging. The experimental results are convincing, and the method shows promise for practical application. However, the lack of more in-depth ablation studies, and a limited discussion on clinical utility, limits its overall impact. Considering these factors, a score of 7 is warranted. This reflects the significant technical contribution, the strong experimental results, and the potential for future influence, balanced by the identified weaknesses.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Policy Disruption in Reinforcement Learning:Adversarial Attack with Large Language Models and Critical State Identification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18113v1)
- **Authors**: Junyong Jiang, Buwei Tian, Chenxing Xu, Songze Li, Lu Dong
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) has achieved remarkable success in fields like robotics and autonomous driving, but adversarial attacks designed to mislead RL systems remain challenging. Existing approaches often rely on modifying the environment or policy, limiting their practicality. This paper proposes an adversarial attack method in which existing agents in the environment guide the target policy to output suboptimal actions without altering the environment. We propose a reward iteration optimization framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate adversarial rewards explicitly tailored to the vulnerabilities of the target agent, thereby enhancing the effectiveness of inducing the target agent toward suboptimal decision-making. Additionally, a critical state identification algorithm is designed to pinpoint the target agent's most vulnerable states, where suboptimal behavior from the victim leads to significant degradation in overall performance. Experimental results in diverse environments demonstrate the superiority of our method over existing approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ARCS, a novel adversarial attack framework targeting reinforcement learning (RL) agents. ARCS aims to disrupt target policies without directly modifying the environment, instead leveraging existing agents to guide the target towards suboptimal actions. The framework incorporates two key modules: (1) an LLM-guided reward iteration optimization, which creates adversarial rewards tailored to the target's vulnerabilities; and (2) a critical state identification mechanism that pinpoints the target's most vulnerable states, further refining the attack strategy. Experimental results across various environments demonstrate ARCS's superiority over existing adversarial policy training methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integration of LLMs for adaptive reward generation and its critical state identification mechanism within an adversarial RL setting. While adversarial attacks in RL are a well-trodden area, the use of LLMs for reward crafting is a relatively new and promising direction. The critical state identification also provides a more targeted approach compared to generic adversarial methods. The combination of these two elements represents a novel contribution.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial limitation of existing adversarial attack methods: the reliance on direct environment or policy manipulation, which is often unrealistic in real-world scenarios. By focusing on indirect influence through multi-agent interaction, ARCS increases the practicality of adversarial attacks. The effectiveness of the framework, as demonstrated through extensive experiments, underscores its potential significance in understanding and mitigating vulnerabilities in RL systems. Furthermore, the focus on critical states identifies areas where the RL agent is most vulnerable and, thus, offers potentially targeted interventions to improve its robustness.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The LLM-guided reward optimization is a clever approach that allows for task-specific and victim-specific attacks, overcoming limitations of generic attack objectives.
    *   The critical state identification mechanism is well-motivated and demonstrably effective in improving attack performance.
    *   The paper is well-written, with clear explanations of the methodology and experimental setup.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of ARCS over existing baselines.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on LLMs for reward generation could be a limitation in resource-constrained environments or scenarios where LLM APIs are inaccessible. While the paper indicates rapid convergence of the LLM reward engineering process, further analysis of the LLM's computational cost and API dependency would strengthen the work.
    *   The study of the method is confined to simulation, and further research is needed to demonstrate its effectiveness in real-world systems. The realism of the MuJoCo and driving environments should also be addressed.
    *   While the method outperforms existing baselines, there might be other advanced, and more robust techniques that could be included as comparison (e.g., those involving uncertainty quantification).
    *   The ablation study could be more comprehensive. For example, the specific contribution of the fine-tuning stage (without the optimized reward) could be further isolated.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in adversarial RL by demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs for adaptive reward design and critical state identification. It could also inspire new methods for improving the robustness of RL systems against indirect adversarial attacks.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of adversarial RL by integrating LLMs and critical state identification to improve the practicality and effectiveness of attacks. The experimental results are convincing and support the claims. However, the reliance on LLMs, the limitation to simulation, and scope for further ablation study modestly constrain the overall impact. The paper opens exciting avenues for future research in adaptive adversarial attacks and robustness enhancement in RL.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### NoCode-bench: A Benchmark for Evaluating Natural Language-Driven Feature Addition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18130v1)
- **Authors**: Le Deng, Zhonghao Jiang, Jialun Cao, Michael Pradel, Zhongxin Liu
- **Abstract**: Natural language-driven no-code development allows users to specify software functionality using natural language (NL) instead of editing source code, promising increased productivity and democratized development. Large language models (LLMs) show potential in enabling this paradigm. In this context, software documentation acts as an NL specification for functionality. This work introduces NoCode-bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate LLMs on real-world NL-driven feature addition tasks, consisting of 634 tasks across 10 projects and 114k code changes. Each task pairs documentation updates with corresponding code implementations, validated by developer-written test cases. A subset of 114 high-quality, human-verified instances, NoCode-bench Verified, ensures reliable evaluation. Our experiments reveal that, despite high token usage, the best LLMs achieve a task success rate of only 15.79%, highlighting challenges in cross-file editing, codebase understanding, and tool calling. These findings indicate that LLMs are not yet ready for fully NL-driven no-code development. NoCode-bench lays the foundation for future advances in this area.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NoCode-bench, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in performing no-code feature addition to existing software projects. The benchmark focuses on a realistic scenario where users specify new software functionality by updating documentation in natural language, and LLMs automatically generate the corresponding code changes. NoCode-bench comprises 634 real-world tasks extracted from open-source project release notes, linked to the relevant code modifications validated by developer-written test cases.  The authors also create a high-quality subset called NoCode-bench Verified.  Experiments using state-of-the-art LLMs reveal limitations in cross-file editing, understanding codebase structure, and tool-calling abilities, indicating that current LLMs are not yet ready for this task. The benchmark aims to stimulate further research in NL-driven no-code software development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a significant gap in existing LLM evaluation benchmarks. While existing benchmarks like SWE-bench focus on bug fixing or issue resolution, NoCode-bench specifically targets the important and prevalent task of feature addition in a no-code development setting, using a novel methodology (documentation changes as input).
*   **Realism and Relevance:** By extracting tasks directly from open-source project release notes and associating them with validated code changes and tests, the benchmark creates a realistic and relevant evaluation scenario that mirrors real-world software development practices.
*   **Comprehensive Construction Pipeline:** The five-phase construction pipeline is systematic and well-defined, encompassing project selection, instance collection, environment construction, instance filtering, and input refinement, ensuring the quality and fidelity of the benchmark. The creation of NoCode-bench Verified further strengthens the credibility of the benchmark.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation and Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of several state-of-the-art LLMs, analyzing their performance using various metrics and identifying key factors that affect model performance, such as limitations in cross-file editing, codebase understanding, and tool-calling abilities.  This includes both quantitative and qualitative perspectives.
*   **Clear Articulation of Challenges:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges faced by LLMs in performing no-code feature addition, providing valuable insights for future research directions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope (Project Diversity):** While the use of open-source Python projects with structured release notes ensures data quality, it also restricts project diversity.  Focusing on other languages or projects without structured notes could enhance generalizability, although that might create additional noise.
*   **Dependency on Developer-Written Tests:** The benchmark relies on existing test cases for evaluation, which may not always be comprehensive or cover all aspects of the new feature. Although acknowledged, this could potentially affect the accuracy of the evaluation, requiring further exploration into automatic test generation.
*   **Potential for Data Leakage:** The instances in NoCode-bench are collected from historical commits in specific GitHub repositories. As GitHub data are widely used to train state-of-the-art LLMs, there is a potential risk of data leakage. The authors did mask PR numbers, but other information might have been memorized by the models.

**Significance:**

NoCode-bench represents a significant contribution to the field by:

*   **Addressing an important and previously unaddressed area of LLM evaluation:** This stimulates research towards practical no-code development solutions.
*   **Providing a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners:** Enables systematic evaluation and comparison of different LLMs on a realistic no-code feature addition task.
*   **Guiding future research directions:** By identifying the key challenges faced by LLMs, the benchmark helps focus research efforts on areas such as cross-file editing, codebase understanding, and tool-calling capabilities.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, the paper presents a significant contribution to the field of LLM-based software engineering. While the limitations in project scope and test coverage are noteworthy, they are outweighed by the novelty, realism, and thoroughness of the benchmark. The paper identifies a gap in existing benchmarks and provides a valuable resource for advancing research in NL-driven no-code development. The detailed analysis of LLM performance provides valuable insights for future research directions. While there's room for improvement in terms of project diversity and evaluation methodology, the benchmark makes a substantial contribution to the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### MathOPEval: A Fine-grained Evaluation Benchmark for Visual Operations of MLLMs in Mathematical Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18140v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyuan Li, Moxin Li, Wenjie Wang, Rui Men, Yichang Zhang, Fuli Feng, Dayiheng Liu, Junyang Lin
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) has enabled step-by-step multi-modal mathematical reasoning by performing visual operations based on the textual instructions. A promising approach uses code as an intermediate representation to precisely express and manipulate the images in the reasoning steps. However, existing evaluations focus mainly on text-only reasoning outputs, leaving the MLLM's ability to perform accurate visual operations via code largely unexplored. This work takes a first step toward addressing that gap by evaluating MLLM's code-based capabilities in multi-modal mathematical reasoning.Specifically, our framework focuses on two key evaluation aspects: (1) Multi-modal Code Generation (MCG) evaluates the model's ability to accurately understand and construct visualizations from scratch. (2) Multi-modal Code Editing (MCE) assesses the model's capacity for fine-grained operations, which include three types: Deletion, Modification and Annotation. To evaluate the above tasks, we incorporate a dataset that covers the five most popular types of mathematical figures, including geometric diagrams, function plots, and three types of statistical charts, to provide a comprehensive and effective measurement of existing MLLMs. Our experimental evaluation involves nine mainstream MLLMs, and the results reveal that existing models still lag significantly behind human performance in performing fine-grained visual operations.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "MATHOPEVAL: A FINE-GRAINED EVALUATION BENCHMARK FOR VISUAL OPERATIONS OF MLLMS IN MATHEMATICAL REASONING":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MathOPEval, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in performing visual operations within mathematical reasoning tasks. The benchmark focuses on two key aspects: Multi-modal Code Generation (MCG) and Multi-modal Code Editing (MCE). MCE is further divided into deletion, modification, and annotation tasks. The benchmark covers five popular mathematical figure types (geometric diagrams, function plots, and three statistical chart types). The authors manually curate a dataset, create multiple-choice and open-ended questions, and implement a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) evaluation strategy. Experiments are conducted on nine mainstream MLLMs, revealing significant performance gaps compared to human performance and highlighting the challenges current models face in fine-grained visual operations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's primary novelty lies in its creation of a fine-grained benchmark specifically targeting the visual operation capabilities of MLLMs in mathematical reasoning. Existing benchmarks often focus on text-only outputs or overall solution accuracy, neglecting the crucial aspect of how well MLLMs can manipulate and understand visual information through code. While the idea of using code as an intermediate representation isn't entirely new, MathOPEval's systematic approach to evaluating code generation and editing for various mathematical figures is a significant contribution. The breakdown of visual operations into deletion, modification, and annotation provides a more granular assessment than previous efforts.

**Significance:**

The MathOPEval benchmark addresses a crucial gap in evaluating MLLMs' abilities. Accurate visual operations are vital for tasks requiring multi-modal mathematical reasoning, as they enable MLLMs to understand and manipulate visual information, aligning it with textual instructions. The paper demonstrates that current MLLMs struggle with these fine-grained operations, revealing limitations that need to be addressed. The dataset and evaluation framework can serve as a valuable resource for future research, guiding the development of more robust and capable MLLMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of evaluating MLLMs' visual operation capabilities and presents a well-motivated solution.
*   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** MathOPEval covers a range of mathematical figure types and visual operations, providing a comprehensive assessment.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation Methodology:** The authors implement a CoT evaluation strategy and conduct human evaluation to ensure reliability.
*   **Informative Experimental Results:** The experiments reveal significant performance gaps and highlight the challenges faced by current MLLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Code Types:** The benchmark primarily focuses on Python and Latex, which might not represent the full spectrum of code representations used in visual reasoning.
*   **Automated Evaluation Reliance:** While human evaluation is performed, the study mostly relies on automated scoring using an LLM, which might introduce bias or inconsistencies. The extent to which the automated scoring reflects true human performance is not clearly quantified.
*   **Generality:** Although five graphical types are assessed, whether these 5 cover enough ground to be considered a general evaluation is questionable.

**Potential Influence:**

MathOPEval has the potential to significantly influence research in the area of multi-modal mathematical reasoning. The benchmark can be used to:

*   Identify the strengths and weaknesses of different MLLMs in performing visual operations.
*   Guide the development of new architectures and training strategies for MLLMs.
*   Track progress in improving MLLMs' visual reasoning capabilities over time.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field by providing a well-designed and comprehensive benchmark for evaluating MLLMs' visual operation capabilities. While there are some weaknesses, the strengths outweigh these limitations. This work's novelty and importance warrant a higher score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### HIVMedQA: Benchmarking large language models for HIV medical decision support
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18143v1)
- **Authors**: Gonzalo Cardenal Antolin, Jacques Fellay, Bashkim Jaha, Roger Kouyos, Niko Beerenwinkel, Diane Duroux
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are emerging as valuable tools to support clinicians in routine decision-making. HIV management is a compelling use case due to its complexity, including diverse treatment options, comorbidities, and adherence challenges. However, integrating LLMs into clinical practice raises concerns about accuracy, potential harm, and clinician acceptance. Despite their promise, AI applications in HIV care remain underexplored, and LLM benchmarking studies are scarce. This study evaluates the current capabilities of LLMs in HIV management, highlighting their strengths and limitations. We introduce HIVMedQA, a benchmark designed to assess open-ended medical question answering in HIV care. The dataset consists of curated, clinically relevant questions developed with input from an infectious disease physician. We evaluated seven general-purpose and three medically specialized LLMs, applying prompt engineering to enhance performance. Our evaluation framework incorporates both lexical similarity and an LLM-as-a-judge approach, extended to better reflect clinical relevance. We assessed performance across key dimensions: question comprehension, reasoning, knowledge recall, bias, potential harm, and factual accuracy. Results show that Gemini 2.5 Pro consistently outperformed other models across most dimensions. Notably, two of the top three models were proprietary. Performance declined as question complexity increased. Medically fine-tuned models did not always outperform general-purpose ones, and larger model size was not a reliable predictor of performance. Reasoning and comprehension were more challenging than factual recall, and cognitive biases such as recency and status quo were observed. These findings underscore the need for targeted development and evaluation to ensure safe, effective LLM integration in clinical care.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HIVMedQA, a new benchmark dataset for evaluating large language models (LLMs) in the context of HIV medical decision support. The authors curate a set of HIV-related questions, validated by an infectious disease physician, and use them to assess the performance of seven general-purpose and three medically specialized LLMs. The evaluation considers several dimensions including question comprehension, reasoning, knowledge recall, bias, potential harm, and factual accuracy.  They explore various prompt engineering techniques and scoring metrics, including LLM-as-a-judge (MedGPT) and lexical similarity measures (MedSynF1), which are then compared. The key findings indicate that Gemini 2.5 Pro generally outperforms other models, medically fine-tuned models don't always surpass general-purpose ones, reasoning and comprehension are more challenging for LLMs than knowledge recall, and models are susceptible to cognitive biases.  They emphasize the importance of nuanced evaluation beyond factual accuracy and the need for better model development strategies to ensure safe and effective integration of LLMs in clinical decision support.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates novelty in several key areas:

*   **Benchmark Dataset:** HIVMedQA itself is a novel contribution. Existing medical QA datasets often lack the specific focus on HIV management, a complex domain requiring integration of diverse clinical information. The inclusion of questions designed to elicit cognitive biases is another novel aspect.
*   **Evaluation Methodology:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation framework combining traditional lexical similarity measures with the LLM-as-a-judge approach (MedGPT). It also carefully examines and improves the LLM-as-a-judge prompts and scoring rubrics. The comparative analysis of different metrics is significant.
*   **Comparative Analysis:** The systematic comparison of general-purpose and medically specialized LLMs is valuable. The finding that specialized models don't consistently outperform general models challenges common assumptions and points to the importance of other factors beyond domain-specific fine-tuning.
*   **Bias and Harm Assessment:** The inclusion of bias detection and harm potential as key evaluation dimensions is crucial, especially in the context of healthcare AI.
*   **Focus on Curbside Consults:** By focusing specifically on the use case of curbside consults, the authors narrow the scope in order to facilitate greater depth, providing real insight into this specific application in a medical setting.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant for the following reasons:

*   **Addressing a Gap:** It fills a critical gap in the evaluation of LLMs for clinical applications. Benchmarking studies in this area are still relatively scarce, and HIV management presents a particularly compelling and complex use case.
*   **Informing Model Development:** The findings provide actionable insights for future model development, highlighting the need for improved reasoning capabilities, bias mitigation strategies, and robust evaluation methodologies. The conclusions regarding the limitations of specialized fine-tuning are particularly important.
*   **Guiding Clinical Implementation:** The paper offers valuable guidance for clinicians and healthcare organizations considering the integration of LLMs into clinical decision support systems. The identification of potential biases and limitations underscores the need for careful oversight and validation.
*   **Advancing Evaluation Methods:** By creating and improving evaluation methods for this particular niche, the authors provide more information to other researchers looking to create similar evaluations.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-Defined Scope:** Focusing on HIV management allows for a more in-depth and relevant evaluation.
*   **Expert Validation:** Involving an infectious disease physician ensures the clinical validity of the questions and answers.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The multi-dimensional evaluation framework captures a wide range of relevant aspects.
*   **Rigorous Methodology:** The paper employs a systematic and well-documented methodology.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The results are clearly presented and well-supported by data.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Dataset Size:** While the dataset is curated, a larger dataset could provide more statistically robust results.
*   **Dependency on GPT-4:** The MedGPT score relies on GPT-4, which is a proprietary model and could be subject to changes or limitations. Also, a model using GPT-4 to evaluate other LLMs introduces the possibility of bias.
*   **Limited Exploration of Mitigation Strategies:** While the paper identifies cognitive biases, it does not explore specific mitigation strategies.
*   **Single-Turn Interactions:** As acknowledged by the authors, the evaluation focuses on single-turn interactions, which may not fully reflect real-world clinical scenarios.
*   **No Human Validation of MedGPT Scores:** While the rephrased answers helped confirm it aligns with answer quality, there was no human comparison of MedGPT scores vs what humans would expect for similar clinical cases.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   **Serving as a benchmark:** HIVMedQA can serve as a valuable benchmark for evaluating future LLMs in the context of HIV management.
*   **Guiding model development:** The findings can inform the development of more robust and reliable LLMs for clinical decision support.
*   **Promoting responsible AI adoption:** By highlighting the limitations and potential risks of LLMs, the paper promotes responsible AI adoption in healthcare.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of medical AI by providing a novel benchmark dataset and a comprehensive evaluation framework for LLMs in the context of HIV management. The findings offer actionable insights for model development and highlight the importance of nuanced evaluation beyond factual accuracy. While the dataset size and reliance on GPT-4 represent minor limitations, the overall novelty and significance of the work justify a score of 8. The study's focus on a complex clinical domain and its comprehensive approach to evaluation make it a valuable resource for researchers and clinicians alike.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### When Noisy Labels Meet Class Imbalance on Graphs: A Graph Augmentation Method with LLM and Pseudo Label
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18153v1)
- **Authors**: Riting Xia, Rucong Wang, Yulin Liu, Anchen Li, Xueyan Liu, Yan Zhang
- **Abstract**: Class-imbalanced graph node classification is a practical yet underexplored research problem. Although recent studies have attempted to address this issue, they typically assume clean and reliable labels when processing class-imbalanced graphs. This assumption often violates the nature of real-world graphs, where labels frequently contain noise. Given this gap, this paper systematically investigates robust node classification for class-imbalanced graphs with noisy labels. We propose GraphALP, a novel Graph Augmentation framework based on Large language models (LLMs) and Pseudo-labeling techniques. Specifically, we design an LLM-based oversampling method to generate synthetic minority nodes, producing label-accurate minority nodes to alleviate class imbalance. Based on the class-balanced graphs, we develop a dynamically weighted pseudo-labeling method to obtain high-confidence pseudo labels to reduce label noise ratio. Additionally, we implement a secondary LLM-guided oversampling mechanism to mitigate potential class distribution skew caused by pseudo labels. Experimental results show that GraphALP achieves superior performance over state-of-the-art methods on class-imbalanced graphs with noisy labels.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the under-explored problem of node classification in class-imbalanced graphs with noisy labels.  It proposes a novel graph augmentation framework called GraphALP, which leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) and pseudo-labeling techniques. GraphALP generates synthetic minority nodes using an LLM-based oversampling method, aiming to alleviate class imbalance while generating accurate labels. It also incorporates a dynamically weighted pseudo-labeling method to reduce the impact of label noise and a secondary LLM-guided oversampling mechanism to correct class skew introduced by pseudo labels.  The authors demonstrate through experiments on real-world datasets that GraphALP outperforms state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty lies in the combination of LLMs and pseudo-labeling within a graph augmentation framework specifically designed for *both* class imbalance and label noise.  While previous works have tackled these issues separately, the paper explicitly recognizes and addresses their synergistic effect.  The use of LLMs for generating synthetic nodes is a relatively new approach in graph learning and differentiates it from traditional oversampling techniques like SMOTE. However, the LLM prompting and integration might not be entirely original as other recent papers have explored using LLMs for graph augmentation in different contexts (e.g., for text-attributed graphs or knowledge graph completion, though perhaps not in this specific intersection of challenges).
The dynamically weighted pseudo labeling is a good idea and appears to be one of the important components of success for handling noisy labels.

**Significance:**

The significance stems from the practical relevance of the problem it addresses. Real-world graphs often exhibit both class imbalance and label noise, rendering existing methods less effective.  A robust solution capable of handling both these issues would be highly valuable. The empirical results, demonstrating superior performance on benchmark datasets, support the claim of significance.  The ablation studies further highlight the importance of each component of the proposed framework.
The analysis, particularly the investigation of how class imbalance and label noise interact, strengthens the paper's contribution. The runtime analysis is also useful to show that the approach can scale.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines and motivates a practically relevant problem.
*   **Comprehensive Approach:**  GraphALP tackles both class imbalance and label noise in a cohesive manner.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate consistent improvements over baselines on several datasets.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each module.
*   **Visualization and Analysis:** The visualizations and additional analysis, such as runtime analysis, bolster the findings.
*   **LLM-Driven Node Generation:** Using LLMs for synthesizing semantically-rich nodes is a promising approach.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Prompting Details:** While the use of LLMs is a key aspect, the paper could benefit from more detailed information about the prompt engineering process.  Specifically, showcasing the impact of different prompt designs. Without this information, the replicability of the LLM component could be a challenge.
*   **Computational Cost:**  While the paper includes a runtime analysis, a more explicit discussion of the computational cost associated with using LLMs, especially for larger graphs, would be beneficial. This needs to be balanced against the benefits of performance, particularly how much performance can be obtained in lower cost LLMs.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper should address the sensitivity of the results to hyperparameter settings (e.g., confidence threshold for pseudo-labeling, imbalance ratio).
*   **Generalization of the synthetic nodes:** A more thorough exploration of the diversity and quality of the synthetic nodes generated by the LLM is needed. Are they truly representative of the minority class, or do they introduce new biases or artifacts?

**Potential Influence:**

If the approach proves to be robust and scalable, it could influence future research in graph learning by:

*   Encouraging the development of more realistic models that account for both class imbalance and label noise.
*   Promoting the use of LLMs for data augmentation in graph settings, leading to new architectures.
*   Inspiring new pseudo-labeling strategies that are aware of class distributions and noise levels.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and technically sound solution to a practically relevant problem. The integration of LLMs for graph augmentation is novel, and the empirical results are compelling. The weaknesses, such as the lack of detail regarding prompt engineering, while not negligible, do not detract significantly from the overall contribution.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** While it represents a significant step forward in addressing class imbalance and label noise in graphs by combining LLMs and pseudo labeling in a novel manner, the score is not higher due to the relatively incremental improvements to LLM prompting in the graph domain, and also due to limited details around prompt engineering and potentially high computational costs. Also, as LLMs continue to improve rapidly, any improvements based on current models may soon be outdated. The code release and more in-depth exploration of design choices would bolster the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Decoupling Knowledge and Reasoning in LLMs: An Exploration Using Cognitive Dual-System Theory
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18178v1)
- **Authors**: Mutian Yang, Jiandong Gao, Ji Wu
- **Abstract**: While large language models (LLMs) leverage both knowledge and reasoning during inference, the capacity to distinguish between them plays a pivotal role in model analysis, interpretability, and development. Inspired by dual-system cognitive theory, we propose a cognition attribution framework to decouple the contribution of knowledge and reasoning. In particular, the cognition of LLMs is decomposed into two distinct yet complementary phases: knowledge retrieval (Phase 1) and reasoning adjustment (Phase 2). To separate these phases, LLMs are prompted to generate answers under two different cognitive modes, fast thinking and slow thinking, respectively. The performance under different cognitive modes is analyzed to quantify the contribution of knowledge and reasoning. This architecture is employed to 15 LLMs across 3 datasets. Results reveal: (1) reasoning adjustment is domain-specific, benefiting reasoning-intensive domains (e.g., mathematics, physics, and chemistry) and potentially imparing knowledge-intensive domains. (2) Parameter scaling improves both knowledge and reasoning, with knowledge improvements being more pronounced. Additionally, parameter scaling make LLMs reasoning significantly more prudent, while moderately more intelligent. (3) Knowledge primarily resides in lower network layers, while reasoning operates in higher layers. Our framework not only helps understand LLMs from a "decoupling" perspective, but also provides new insights into existing research, including scaling laws, hierarchical knowledge editing, and limitations of small-model reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a "cognition attribution framework" to decouple the contributions of knowledge retrieval and reasoning in large language models (LLMs). Inspired by dual-system cognitive theory (fast thinking vs. slow thinking), the framework prompts LLMs to generate answers in two distinct cognitive modes.  Fast thinking relies primarily on knowledge retrieval, while slow thinking engages both knowledge retrieval and reasoning adjustment (through Chain-of-Thought generation). By comparing performance under these two modes, the framework attempts to quantify the contribution of each. The framework is applied to 15 LLMs across 3 datasets, revealing insights into domain-specific reasoning abilities, the impact of parameter scaling on knowledge and reasoning, and the hierarchical organization of knowledge and reasoning within the LLM's network layers. The paper also explores correction versus overthinking during the reasoning adjustment phase.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using the dual-system cognitive theory as a lens for analyzing LLM behavior is interesting and provides a structured way to think about the knowledge versus reasoning capabilities. Decomposing LLM cognition into knowledge retrieval and reasoning adjustment phases is a reasonable simplification. The specific methodology of using fast vs. slow thinking prompts to isolate these phases has some merit. The experiments exploring correction vs. overthinking and the layer-wise CKA analysis adds further nuances.

*   **Significance:** The insights derived from the experiments are potentially valuable. The paper claims to shed light on scaling laws, hierarchical knowledge editing, and the limitations of small-model reasoning. Showing how reasoning benefits vary across domains is an important observation, providing justification for the argument that reasoning is a vital component of mathematics, physics, and chemistry, where it offers value by reducing ambiguity and correcting mistakes. The discussion of "prudence" (avoiding overthinking) as a key benefit of parameter scaling is also noteworthy. The suggestion that knowledge resides in lower layers, while reasoning occurs in higher layers aligns with existing knowledge and offers insight into network architecture.

*   **Weaknesses and Limitations:**

    *   **Oversimplification:** The dual-system cognitive theory is a simplification of human cognition, and mapping it directly to LLMs might be an overreach. The separation of knowledge retrieval and reasoning might not be so clear-cut in practice. The division into Phase 1 and Phase 2 may be a somewhat artificial construct.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Bias:** The results are heavily dependent on the effectiveness of the fast/slow thinking prompts. Designing prompts that truly elicit only knowledge retrieval in the "fast thinking" mode is challenging. There's a risk of the prompts themselves influencing the reasoning process.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The accuracy-based metrics (CKR, CRA) provide a high-level view, but don't delve into the *quality* of reasoning. Chain-of-thought evaluation, even with an LLM as evaluator, can be subjective.
    *   **Generalizability:** The choice of datasets and LLMs can influence the findings. While the paper uses a decent variety of models, the results might not generalize to all LLM architectures or problem types.
    *   **Causality:** The study establishes correlations but does not necessarily prove causality. For example, the layer-wise CKA analysis suggests a functional separation, but it doesn't demonstrate that reasoning *only* happens in higher layers.
    *   **Missing metrics and calculations:** Detailed explanations of the calculation of each of the proposed metrics and the derivation of the formula are sometimes missing from the main text.

*   **Impact:** If the framework and findings hold up under further scrutiny, the work could influence how we analyze and understand LLM behavior. It may encourage more research on the interplay between knowledge and reasoning, and guide the development of more targeted training strategies.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel framework for analyzing knowledge and reasoning in LLMs, inspired by a well-established cognitive theory. While the framework involves some simplification and the experimental results are subject to potential biases (especially in prompt engineering), the work offers a structured approach to a challenging problem. The insights related to domain-specific reasoning, the impact of scaling, and the hierarchical organization of knowledge and reasoning are valuable contributions. The paper also discusses error correction and overthinking, which is not often included in LLM studies. The experiments cover multiple LLMs, enhancing generalizability. The work’s potential impact would be in encouraging further research on the interplay between knowledge and reasoning and guiding the development of more targeted training strategies. However, due to the described limitations (especially simplification and potential for bias), the overall contribution is modest.
Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### SCOPE: Stochastic and Counterbiased Option Placement for Evaluating Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18182v1)
- **Authors**: Wonjun Jeong, Dongseok Kim, Taegkeun Whangbo
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) can achieve inflated scores on multiple-choice tasks by exploiting inherent biases in option positions or labels, rather than demonstrating genuine understanding. This study introduces SCOPE, an evaluation framework designed to measure and mitigate such selection bias in a dataset-independent manner. By repeatedly invoking a null prompt that lacks semantic content, SCOPE estimates each model's unique position-bias distribution. It then redistributes the answer slot according to the inverse-bias distribution, thereby equalizing the lucky-rate, the probability of selecting the correct answer by chance. Furthermore, it prevents semantically similar distractors from being placed adjacent to the answer, thereby blocking near-miss guesses based on superficial proximity cues. Across multiple benchmark experiments, SCOPE consistently outperformed existing debiasing methods in terms of stable performance improvements and showed clearer confidence distributions over correct options. This framework thus offers a new standard for enhancing the fairness and reliability of LLM evaluations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SCOPE, a novel evaluation framework for Large Language Models (LLMs) designed to mitigate the impact of selection biases and shortcut strategies in multiple-choice question answering. SCOPE consists of two main modules: Inverse-Positioning (IP) and Semantic-Spread (SS). The IP module estimates a model's position bias by using null prompts and then redistributes answer choices based on the inverse of this bias. The SS module identifies semantically similar distractors and disperses them probabilistically away from the correct answer to prevent near-miss guessing. The paper demonstrates that SCOPE consistently outperforms existing debiasing methods across multiple benchmarks, showing more stable performance improvements and clearer confidence distributions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its dataset-independent approach to estimating and mitigating selection bias. Unlike previous methods that modify the dataset (e.g., shuffling answer positions), SCOPE estimates bias directly from the model's behavior with null prompts. This is a crucial distinction because it aims to capture the model's intrinsic biases, rather than those induced by a specific evaluation environment. The combination of IP and SS modules to address both position bias and semantic similarity-based shortcuts is also novel.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is considerable. Selection biases in LLMs can lead to inflated accuracy scores, masking the true understanding capabilities of the models. A reliable and fair evaluation framework like SCOPE is essential for making informed comparisons between models and for understanding their limitations. By providing a mechanism to remove bias, the authors contribute to a more accurate assessment of LLMs. Furthermore, the ability to identify and mitigate different kinds of biases (positional and semantic) has significant practical implications for ensuring the trustworthy deployment of LLMs in real-world applications. The interpretability metrics (Answer and Distractor F1 scores) derived from repeated trials are a welcome addition, allowing researchers to delve deeper into a model's internal consistency and potential misconceptions.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset-Independent Bias Estimation:** This is a major strength. Estimating bias using null prompts is a clever and generalizable technique.
    *   **Comprehensive Debasing:** The framework addresses both position bias and semantic shortcutting.
    *   **Theoretical Grounding:** The paper provides a theoretical analysis demonstrating how SCOPE cancels out position bias and spreads semantically similar distractors.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The framework is rigorously evaluated across multiple benchmarks (MMLU and CSQA) and LLMs.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide code and instructions for reproducing their experiments.
    *   **Interpretability:** The proposed method offers interpretable metrics for evaluating the source of error.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** Repeated calls with null prompts can be computationally expensive, especially for proprietary API-based models. The paper acknowledges this limitation and suggests adaptive sampling as a potential solution.
    *   **Surface-Level Biases:** The framework might not fully address surface-level biases such as those related to input length or word frequency.
    *   **Embedding Quality Dependence:** The effectiveness of semantic dispersion depends on the quality of the Sentence-BERT embeddings, which might be weaker in specific domains. The influence of the model used for evaluating semantic similarity is unknown.
    *   **Distractor F1 Rise on CSQA:** Distractor F1 does rise for the CSQA benchmark, suggesting further risk-aware calibration might be needed.
    *   **The assumption of the consistency in model responses can be challenged.** A LLM might perform better with randomization and a stochastic approach, which might be penalized by SCOPE.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence how LLMs are evaluated. SCOPE provides a new standard for fairness and reliability in LLM evaluations, which could lead to more trustworthy comparisons between models. Future research might build on this work by incorporating multi-bias mitigation techniques, domain-adaptive embeddings, and confidence calibration strategies. It's highly likely the approach, or components of it, will be integrated into existing evaluation pipelines.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a novel, theoretically sound, and empirically validated framework for addressing an important problem in LLM evaluation. While it has some limitations, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, and it has the potential to become a standard practice for assessing language models. The fact the paper provides interpretable metrics and allows easy identification of a source of bias makes it a significant contribution that goes beyond addressing the accuracy issue. The careful ablation studies further contribute to the trustworthiness of the proposed method.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Safeguarding RAG Pipelines with GMTP: A Gradient-based Masked Token Probability Method for Poisoned Document Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18202v1)
- **Authors**: San Kim, Jonghwi Kim, Yejin Jeon, Gary Geunbae Lee
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) enhances Large Language Models (LLMs) by providing external knowledge for accurate and up-to-date responses. However, this reliance on external sources exposes a security risk, attackers can inject poisoned documents into the knowledge base to steer the generation process toward harmful or misleading outputs. In this paper, we propose Gradient-based Masked Token Probability (GMTP), a novel defense method to detect and filter out adversarially crafted documents. Specifically, GMTP identifies high-impact tokens by examining gradients of the retriever's similarity function. These key tokens are then masked, and their probabilities are checked via a Masked Language Model (MLM). Since injected tokens typically exhibit markedly low masked-token probabilities, this enables GMTP to easily detect malicious documents and achieve high-precision filtering. Experiments demonstrate that GMTP is able to eliminate over 90% of poisoned content while retaining relevant documents, thus maintaining robust retrieval and generation performance across diverse datasets and adversarial settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GMTP (Gradient-based Masked Token Probability), a novel defense mechanism against corpus poisoning attacks in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) pipelines. GMTP identifies high-impact tokens based on retriever gradient analysis and assesses their predictability via a Masked Language Model (MLM). Adversarially injected tokens often exhibit low masked token probabilities, allowing GMTP to filter out poisoned documents with high precision while maintaining retrieval and generation performance. The authors demonstrate GMTP's effectiveness across various datasets, adversarial settings, and hyperparameter configurations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The idea of using retriever gradients to identify key tokens and then leveraging MLM probability to detect adversarial inputs presents some novelty.  Previous defenses often relied on perplexity or other general text quality measures, which this method attempts to refine by focusing on tokens critical to the retrieval process.  The combination of these two elements -- gradient-based token selection and MLM probability scoring -- seems to be a relatively new approach.

**Significance:** Corpus poisoning is a significant and growing threat to RAG systems. A robust defense is crucial for ensuring the reliability and trustworthiness of these systems. The paper demonstrates strong empirical results showing that GMTP can filter out poisoned documents effectively while preserving retrieval performance. This has practical importance for deploying RAG in real-world applications.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents extensive experimental validation across multiple datasets and attack scenarios. GMTP consistently outperforms baseline methods in filtering rate and maintains retrieval quality.
*   **Robustness:** GMTP demonstrates robustness across various hyperparameter settings, retrieval models (DPR, Contriever, ColBERT), and LLMs (BERT, RoBERTa). This suggests that the approach is generalizable and adaptable to different RAG configurations.
*   **Efficiency:** GMTP relies on lightweight computations using smaller models like BERT, making it computationally efficient compared to generation-based defense mechanisms.
*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the vulnerabilities of RAG systems to corpus poisoning attacks and the challenges associated with detecting poisoned documents.
*   **Focus on Cheating Tokens:** While other methods use overall document unnaturalness GMTP attempts to isolate cheating tokens which better reflect the adversarial intent.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Attacks:** The paper focuses on specific types of corpus poisoning attacks, primarily those involving token-level manipulation using Hotflip or similar methods. It acknowledges its limitations in detecting naturally crafted documents with false information or biased news articles. The paper mentions a defense against single injection, or creating separate documents for each query pattern.
*   **Over-Reliance on MLMs:** The reliance on MLMs as the primary detection mechanism could be a limitation. As MLMs continue to improve, adversaries might develop more sophisticated techniques to generate adversarial tokens that are indistinguishable from natural language. Also performance of MLM might vary in different settings.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:** While the paper claims robustness, the selection of N, M, and λ requires careful tuning. The need for domain-dependent thresholding might limit the "out-of-the-box" usability of the method. The results regarding performance of different parameter settings seem somewhat limited.
*   **Limited Evaluation of Generation Quality:**  The ACC and ASR metrics provide some insight into generation performance, a more comprehensive evaluation of the generated responses (e.g., using human evaluation or more sophisticated LLM-based evaluation metrics) would strengthen the paper.
*   **Assumption of Not Being Able to Retrain Models:** A significant assumption is the inability to retrain retriever and generator models. In many real-world scenarios, fine-tuning is a viable option, and defense strategies could potentially adapt to adversarial attacks more effectively if this assumption were relaxed.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper's findings could influence future research on defense mechanisms for RAG systems. The gradient-based token analysis approach could be further explored and refined.  The work could also inspire the development of more robust MLMs that are less susceptible to adversarial manipulation.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and promising defense mechanism with strong empirical results, its limitations in the scope of attacks, reliance on MLMs, parameter sensitivity, and the assumption of not being able to retrain the model keep it from achieving a higher score. GMTP offers a significant step towards more secure RAG pipelines, but further research is needed to address its limitations and develop more comprehensive defense strategies.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Exploring the Impact of Instruction-Tuning on LLM's Susceptibility to Misinformation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18203v1)
- **Authors**: Kyubeen Han, Junseo Jang, Hongjin Kim, Geunyeong Jeong, Harksoo Kim
- **Abstract**: Instruction-tuning enhances the ability of large language models (LLMs) to follow user instructions more accurately, improving usability while reducing harmful outputs. However, this process may increase the model's dependence on user input, potentially leading to the unfiltered acceptance of misinformation and the generation of hallucinations. Existing studies primarily highlight that LLMs are receptive to external information that contradict their parametric knowledge, but little research has been conducted on the direct impact of instruction-tuning on this phenomenon. In our study, we investigate the impact of instruction-tuning on LLM's susceptibility to misinformation. Our analysis reveals that instruction-tuned LLMs are significantly more likely to accept misinformation when it is presented by the user. A comparison with base models shows that instruction-tuning increases reliance on user-provided information, shifting susceptibility from the assistant role to the user role. Furthermore, we explore additional factors influencing misinformation susceptibility, such as the role of the user in prompt structure, misinformation length, and the presence of warnings in the system prompt. Our findings underscore the need for systematic approaches to mitigate unintended consequences of instruction-tuning and enhance the reliability of LLMs in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how instruction-tuning, a technique used to improve the ability of large language models (LLMs) to follow instructions, affects their susceptibility to misinformation.  The authors hypothesize that instruction-tuning, while beneficial in many ways, may increase an LLM's dependence on user input, making it more likely to accept and propagate misinformation even when it conflicts with the LLM's pre-existing knowledge. Through experiments using the Farm dataset and several open-source and proprietary LLMs, the study finds that instruction-tuned LLMs are indeed more susceptible to misinformation presented by the user, especially when the misinformation is presented as a separate turn in a conversation. They also explored the impact of misinformation length and the inclusion of warnings in the system prompt.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a critical and timely question: the potential downsides of instruction tuning. While previous works have studied LLMs' susceptibility to misinformation, this study directly examines the *impact of the instruction-tuning process itself* on this phenomenon. This is a relatively unexplored area and adds value to the field. The finding that instruction tuning shifts the LLM's focus from assistant-provided to user-provided information is a novel insight. However, the use of a dataset with pre-existing misinformation slightly diminishes the novelty; a dataset constructed specifically for analyzing this phenomenon would have been stronger.

*   **Significance:** The findings have significant implications for the development and deployment of LLMs. If instruction-tuning, intended to improve usability and safety, inadvertently increases susceptibility to misinformation, it poses a real-world risk. This study highlights the need for systematic approaches to mitigate these unintended consequences and build more reliable LLMs. The identification of factors like misinformation length and the limited effectiveness of simple warnings provides valuable guidance for future research. The paper prompts further investigation into balancing instruction-following with robust knowledge verification. It provides concrete evidence suggesting that enhancing an LLMs reliability will require a systematic approach to balance the increased propensity to follow instructions derived from instruction tuning processes, as well as the knowledge verification mechanisms required to mitigate hallucinations.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The experimental design is well-structured and includes multiple scenarios to analyze the role of the user and assistant.
    *   The use of both open-source and proprietary LLMs adds robustness to the findings.
    *   The paper clearly articulates its research questions and provides thorough explanations of the methods used.
    *   The analysis of factors influencing misinformation susceptibility, such as the length of the misinformation, adds depth to the study.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The study's reliance on the Farm dataset, while containing misinformation, isn't explicitly designed to study instruction-tuning biases. A more purpose-built dataset could strengthen the conclusions.
    *   The investigation into misinformation warnings is somewhat limited; the study only uses a single simple warning. Exploring more sophisticated warning strategies would be valuable.
    *   The study acknowledges limitations related to the limited scale of open-source models used. Validation with larger models would improve the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The reasons behind the outliers observed in some models (e.g., Qwen2.5-7B) are not fully explained. Further analysis of model architecture and training data might shed light on these discrepancies.
    *   Although the paper mentions the logical, credibility and emotional dimensions to misinformation, the main experimentation focuses on the first type, which somewhat reduces the significance of any possible insights regarding the dimensions, particularly with respect to the generalizability.
    *   The limitations include the absence of the validation for large open-source LLMs. Further analysis is needed to understand how model size affects the susceptibility to misinformation

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in the following areas:

    *   Development of new instruction-tuning techniques that mitigate the risk of misinformation acceptance.
    *   Design of more effective methods for detecting and filtering misinformation in LLM inputs.
    *   Exploration of the interplay between instruction-following and knowledge verification in LLMs.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant investigation into a crucial aspect of LLM safety: the unintended consequences of instruction-tuning. It identifies a clear link between instruction-tuning and increased susceptibility to user-provided misinformation. While the study has some limitations, particularly in dataset design and the depth of the warning investigation, the overall contribution is valuable and will likely stimulate further research in this area. The strengths in the experimental design, range of models evaluated, and detailed analysis of influencing factors outweigh the weaknesses. This study will prompt others to consider a systematic approach to balance the increased propensity to follow instructions with the knowledge verification mechanisms to mitigate hallucinations. Therefore, the paper deserves a score of 7.5, reflecting its clear contribution and likely impact on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Prune&Comp: Free Lunch for Layer-Pruned LLMs via Iterative Pruning with Magnitude Compensation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18212v1)
- **Authors**: Xinrui Chen, Hongxing Zhang, Fanyi Zeng, Yongxian Wei, Yizhi Wang, Xitong Ling, Guanghao Li, Chun Yuan
- **Abstract**: Layer pruning has emerged as a promising technique for compressing large language models (LLMs) while achieving acceleration proportional to the pruning ratio. In this work, we identify that removing any layer induces a significant magnitude gap in hidden states, resulting in substantial performance degradation. To address this issue, we propose Prune&Comp, a novel plug-and-play layer pruning scheme that leverages magnitude compensation to mitigate such gaps in a training-free manner. Specifically, we first estimate the magnitude gap caused by layer removal and then eliminate this gap by rescaling the remaining weights offline, with zero runtime overhead incurred. We further demonstrate the advantages of Prune&Comp through an iterative pruning strategy. When integrated with an iterative prune-and-compensate loop, Prune&Comp consistently enhances existing layer pruning metrics. For instance, when 5 layers of LLaMA-3-8B are pruned using the prevalent block influence metric, Prune&Comp nearly halves the perplexity and retains 93.19\% of the original model's question-answering performance, outperforming the baseline by 4.01%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PRUNE&COMP: Free Lunch for Layer-Pruned LLMs via Iterative Pruning with Magnitude Compensation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of performance degradation in large language models (LLMs) after layer pruning. The authors identify that removing layers creates a significant magnitude gap in the hidden states, which negatively impacts performance.  They propose a method called PRUNE&COMP, which aims to compensate for this gap in a training-free manner. The method estimates the magnitude gap and rescales the weights of remaining layers. They also integrate magnitude compensation into an iterative pruning strategy, showing improvements over standard layer pruning methods.  Experiments on LLaMA-3-8B and Qwen3-8B demonstrate that PRUNE&COMP consistently enhances existing layer pruning metrics, improving both perplexity and question answering performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the identification of the magnitude gap as a primary cause of performance degradation in layer-pruned LLMs, and the proposal of a *training-free* compensation strategy to address it. While layer pruning and iterative pruning are well-established, the explicit focus on this magnitude gap and the simple yet effective compensation mechanism represent a non-trivial contribution. The method also makes no assumptions about the underlying pruning metric and claims to boost the performance for all metrics.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential for improved efficiency in LLM deployment. Layer pruning is valuable because it speeds up inference without requiring specialized hardware or retraining. PRUNE&COMP makes layer pruning *more* effective, increasing the performance of pruned models relative to standard approaches. The claim of "free lunch" is a strong one, suggesting that the technique has minimal overhead.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of performance degradation after layer pruning.
    *   **Simple and Effective Solution:** PRUNE&COMP offers a relatively simple and intuitive solution that can be easily integrated with existing layer pruning methods. It makes it easy for practitioners to adopt the algorithm.
    *   **Training-Free Approach:** The training-free nature of the compensation strategy is a significant advantage, as it avoids the computational cost and complexity of retraining.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation of PRUNE&COMP on multiple LLMs, pruning metrics, and benchmarks.
    *   **Insightful Ablation Study:** The ablation study effectively demonstrates the individual contributions of the magnitude compensation and iterative pruning components.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper demonstrates empirical success, a more in-depth theoretical analysis of *why* the magnitude gap is so critical would strengthen the argument. What are the specific mechanisms by which the magnitude gap affects the subsequent layers/attention mechanisms?
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** While the method is shown to improve performance for LLaMA-3-8B/Qwen3-8B, the effectiveness on truly massive models (e.g., 100B+ parameters) remains an open question. How does the compensation factor 'a' scale with model size? It is important to note that all models mentioned are under the 100 billion parameter threshold.
    *   **Dependence on Calibration Data:** The method relies on a calibration dataset to estimate the magnitude gap. The choice of this dataset can have a significant impact on performance. The paper can include data on how performance varies for different sample datasets.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to Other Compensation Techniques:** The paper focuses on comparing PRUNE&COMP to standard pruning methods. It would be interesting to compare it against other magnitude-based compensation techniques, if any, that exist in the literature.

*   **Potential Impact:** PRUNE&COMP has the potential to become a widely adopted technique for layer pruning in LLMs. Its simplicity and training-free nature make it attractive for practitioners who want to improve the efficiency of their models without incurring significant costs.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and effective approach to improve layer pruning. The discovery of the magnitude gap effect is important, and the PRUNE&COMP provides a simple, training-free method to mitigate this effect. The comprehensive experiments support these claims. However, the lack of strong theoretical justifications, the scalability concerns, and the dependence on calibration data somewhat limit the paper's overall score. Future work should address these limitations to further enhance the contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LEAF: Latent Diffusion with Efficient Encoder Distillation for Aligned Features in Medical Image Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18214v1)
- **Authors**: Qilin Huang, Tianyu Lin, Zhiguang Chen, Fudan Zheng
- **Abstract**: Leveraging the powerful capabilities of diffusion models has yielded quite effective results in medical image segmentation tasks. However, existing methods typically transfer the original training process directly without specific adjustments for segmentation tasks.
